# Sprint 3 — Step model overhaul, mutable plans, location-agnostic execution

Astrid reshape sprint 3. Full design: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/idea.md` § Sprint 3 (lines 214–295). This brief expands the sprint section for execution. **This is a large sprint and the architectural keystone of the reshape — premium/robust/high profile picked deliberately.**

Working directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid`. Start from branch `reshape/sprint-2`; create `reshape/sprint-3` off it and work there.

## Goal

Collapse the step model into one shape (no more `code`/`attested`/`nested` kinds). Make plans editable mid-run with explicit mutation rules. Make execution location-agnostic via three adapters (only `local` and `manual` ship this sprint; `remote-artifact` is reserved and rejected at runtime). Add the `claim` verb. Land the kernel-level pieces (validator, versioned step paths, mutation events, inbox path+version matching) that Sprint 4 + 5a all depend on.

**No orchestrator porting (Sprint 5a/5b territory).** Sprint 3 is the kernel + the adapters; existing packs are migrated forward mechanically.

## Foundational decisions (locked, do not re-debate)

### The new Step shape

```
Step {
  id: str
  version: int                       # bumped when a dispatched step is superseded
  adapter: "local" | "manual" | "remote-artifact"
  requires_ack: bool
  assignee: "system" | "agent:<id>" | "any-agent" | "human:<name>" | "any-human"
  produces: [{name, path, check}]
  repeat: until | for_each | none
  command: str?                      # leaf step: the thing to run; interpreted by adapter
  children: [Step]?                  # group step: nesting; replaces old `nested` kind
  cost: {amount, currency, source}?  # recorded at completion
  superseded_by: {
    to_version: int,
    scope: "all" | "future-iterations" | "future-items"  # REQUIRED; no default
  }?
}
```

- Leaf step has `command`, no `children`.
- Group step has `children`, no `command`. Its `produces` aggregates from descendants.
- `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are **gone** (migrated forward — see Migration).
- `version` starts at 1 for the initial step; bumps to 2+ on supersede.

### Supersession scope is mandatory

A `supersede-step` verb without an explicit `scope` is rejected at the verb. Three scopes:
- `"all"` — aborts in-flight iterations/items and restarts at iter/item 1 of the new version.
- `"future-iterations"` — `repeat.until` only; current iteration finishes against old version, iter+1 uses new version.
- `"future-items"` — `repeat.for_each` only; current item finishes against old version, next item uses new version.

### Plan mutation rules

- `plan.json` is the *initial* plan, written once at run start.
- The *effective* plan = `plan.json` + replayed mutation events from `events.jsonl`.
- **Append-only once dispatched.** A step is "dispatched" the moment `astrid next` returns its payload (event: `step_dispatched`). After dispatch:
  - `remove-step` is rejected. Use `abort` on the run instead, or `supersede-step`.
  - `edit-step` is rejected. Use `supersede-step` to bump version.
  - The original `steps/<id>/v1/` directory is **never deleted**.
- **Undispatched steps** can be added, edited, or removed freely. `remove-step` writes a tombstone event; the cursor skips tombstoned steps.
- **Canonical step path is always versioned**: every step lives at `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`. Iterations and items live under `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/` and `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`.

### The six plan-mutation invariants

Single function (call it `validate_mutation` or similar) runs **all six** invariants against the proposed effective tree:

1. **Schema validation** — full re-validate of the proposed effective tree (not a diff).
2. **Sibling-id uniqueness** at every frame (existing `plan.py:_assert_unique_paths` rule, applied to effective tree).
3. **`produces` reference integrity** — no dangling refs to deleted/tombstoned steps.
4. **Adapter validation** — declared adapter is `local | manual | remote-artifact`, command shape matches adapter (e.g. `manual` cannot have empty `command` string).
5. **Repeat-source validation** — `repeat.for_each.from_ref` resolves to a real prior `produces` in the effective tree.
6. **Lease epoch CAS** — the writer's epoch matches current (Sprint 1's lease).

Mutations that fail **any** check are rejected at the verb. No partial application. The verb returns a non-zero exit with the specific invariant that failed.

### Three adapters; only two ship this sprint

- **`local`** — `command` is a shell command run as a subprocess in the project root. Outputs land in `produces/`. Completion = exit code 0 + produces checks pass.
- **`manual`** — agent (or human) runs the work out-of-band; `command` is the dispatch payload (instructions). The agent is responsible for landing outputs in `produces/`. Completion = `astrid ack` (sync) or completion JSON in inbox (async).
- **`remote-artifact`** — schema-reserved here, **rejected at runtime** in Sprint 3 with: `astrid start / astrid next: step '<id>' declares adapter 'remote-artifact'; not yet implemented (Sprint 5a). Use --adapter local or manual.` Full implementation lands in Sprint 5a alongside the hype port.

Adapters are first-class code, not config.

### Ack identity at ack time, not author time

`astrid ack <step> --decision {approve,retry,iterate,abort} --agent <id>|--actor <name> [--evidence <path>]`. Both `--agent` and `--actor` are required (one or the other) — **no anonymous acks**. Every ack carries the `writer_epoch` it read; stale acks are rejected by the kernel.

### Authoring split

- **Leaf-template** — what we used to call an executor. One concrete unit of work. Same pack layout on disk (`pack/<name>/run.py` + `executor.yaml`).
- **Plan-template** — what we used to call an orchestrator: a reusable composition. Materializes into a step subtree at plan-build or plan-mutation time.

Runtime never sees templates — only the resulting step tree. During Sprint 3, the new template kinds land **alongside** the legacy `OrchestratorDefinition` YAML — both readable. Legacy YAML is materialized into the new step tree on the way in. (Sprint 5b removes legacy YAML acceptance.)

### Capture-everything rule

Anything producing an audit-worthy artifact = a step. The friction is small: `astrid plan add-step --command '...' --produces '...'` + `astrid next`. The alternative — agent runs python ad-hoc, artifact appears with no provenance — is what we're eliminating. Truly ephemeral commands don't need it.

## Deliverables

### Step schema + plan rewrite

- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/plan.py` against the new step shape. Keep `_assert_unique_paths` (or its equivalent in the new code) and the validators for the existing invariants; add what's needed for the new ones.
- Versioned step path everywhere: `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`. Update all path helpers in `astrid/core/task/`.
- **`code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are gone.** Migration script (see below) rewrites existing plans.

### Plan-mutation verbs + event

- `astrid plan add-step` — insert a step into the effective tree at a given path. Position via `--after <step-path>` / `--before <step-path>` / `--into <group-path>`.
- `astrid plan edit-step` — modify an undispatched step (fields specifiable as flags).
- `astrid plan remove-step` — write a tombstone event for an undispatched step. Rejected on dispatched steps.
- `astrid plan supersede-step` — bump version on a dispatched (or any) step. **Requires `--scope {all,future-iterations,future-items}`.**
- All four verbs emit a single `plan_mutated` event in `events.jsonl` carrying: actor, writer_epoch, diff (the specific operation), timestamp. The hash chain extends to plan mutations.

### Plan-mutation validator

- Single entry point that runs all six invariants. Every verb routes through it. Rejection is total.
- Returns structured error: which invariant failed + the specific element that triggered it.
- Test: each invariant has at least one positive (passes) and one negative (rejects with the right error code) case.

### Cursor

- Cursor stores `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` per dispatched step.
- Walks the effective plan respecting tombstones and supersedes (honoring `scope` per repeat semantics).
- `astrid status` shows the cursor's current step at its current version.

### Inbox path+version match

- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` so entries match on `(plan_step_path, step_version, item_id?)` not just the leaf step id.
- Inbox entries from before a supersede event route to the original version's directory; entries after route to the new version.
- Stale entries that target a tombstoned or fully-superseded step move to `.rejected/` (existing pattern — extend it).

### Adapters

- `astrid/core/adapter/local.py` — subprocess-based local execution. Dispatch = spawn subprocess (sync or detached); completion = exit code 0 + produces checks; failure = non-zero exit OR missing produces.
- `astrid/core/adapter/manual.py` — emit dispatch payload (`command` field) to the agent's display path; completion = `astrid ack` event OR completion JSON arriving in inbox.
- `astrid/core/adapter/remote_artifact.py` — stub that rejects at dispatch time with the deferral message above.
- All three implement a common interface (e.g. `Adapter.dispatch(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.poll(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.complete(step, run_ctx)`). Pick the interface to fit how `astrid next` already works.

### Claim verb

- `astrid claim <step> [--for human:<name>] [--for agent:<id>]`. Default `--for` = current session's identity.
- Emits `claim` / `unclaim` events.
- Read-only sessions blocked from claiming.

### `requires_ack` enforcement

- Steps with `requires_ack: true` cannot complete on adapter completion alone; require an `astrid ack` event with a non-stale `writer_epoch`.
- `--agent` or `--actor` is **required** at ack time. Argparse rejects neither/both.

### Mutating verbs include `writer_epoch` + CAS

- Every mutating verb reads the current lease's `writer_epoch` before mutating; bumps it as part of the mutation; rejects if the epoch advanced under it.
- Relies on Sprint 1's lease primitives directly — do NOT reimplement.

### Cost field on completion events

- Each step completion event (`step_completed`, `step_failed`) carries `cost: {amount, currency, source}` if the adapter or executing code declared one.
- Adapters MAY emit cost; local subprocess execution typically won't. The field is optional but the schema reserves it (Sprint 4 + 5a depend on this).

### Inbox-driven async-completion documented + exercised

- Worked example for `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab. Demonstrate that closing the tab, opening a new one, `astrid attach`, `astrid next` picks up wherever inbox left things.
- Worked example for `manual` adapter: out-of-band ack landing as a completion JSON in inbox.
- Documentation lives at `docs/reshape/sprint-3/async-completion.md`.

### Migrations

- **Plan migration** — every existing `plan.json` rewritten to the collapsed schema. Every existing step gets `adapter: local` and `version: 1`. `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds rewritten to the new shape (best-effort mapping; ambiguous cases get `adapter: local` + a TODO note in the diff log).
- **Step path migration** — every existing `steps/<id>/...` directory becomes `steps/<id>/v1/...` in one shot. Run on `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/` (which is currently mostly empty — but the script must be idempotent and exercise the no-op path cleanly).
- Both migrations live under `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/`. `--dry-run` default; `--apply` commits.

### Hype port spike — back half of sprint, throwaway, do NOT merge to reshape/sprint-3

- Scratch branch `reshape/sprint-3-hype-spike` off `reshape/sprint-3`.
- Take the first three steps of `builtin.hype` only (transcribe → cut → render) and port them against the new step model.
- Deliverable is `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` enumerating every schema gap, adapter mismatch, and cost-field ambiguity discovered.
- **Schema revisions resulting from the spike land in Sprint 3, not deferred to a follow-up.** Revise the schema while it's still cheap.
- The spike branch is throwaway — push it for archival but do not merge.

### Tests

- `tests/task/test_plan_schema_collapsed.py` — new step schema validation, including all six invariants positive + negative.
- `tests/task/test_plan_mutation_verbs.py` — add/edit/remove/supersede each succeed in the happy path and reject correctly when post-dispatch / scope-missing / invariant-failing.
- `tests/task/test_step_versioning.py` — supersede produces `v2/`, `v1/` survives, cursor tracks correct version.
- `tests/task/test_cursor_supersede_scopes.py` — all three scopes exercised under repeat.until and repeat.for_each.
- `tests/task/test_inbox_path_version_match.py` — entries route correctly across a supersede event; stale entries land in `.rejected/`.
- `tests/adapter/test_local.py` — subprocess dispatch, completion, failure modes.
- `tests/adapter/test_manual.py` — dispatch payload emission, ack-driven completion, inbox-driven completion.
- `tests/adapter/test_remote_artifact_rejected.py` — `astrid next` rejects cleanly with the deferral message.
- `tests/task/test_claim_unclaim.py` — claim emits event, unclaim emits event, read-only session blocked.
- `tests/task/test_ack_identity_required.py` — argparse rejects no-agent-no-actor; both rejected too.
- `tests/migrations/test_plan_migration.py` — collapsed-schema rewrite across all old step kinds.
- `tests/migrations/test_step_path_migration.py` — `steps/<id>/...` → `steps/<id>/v1/...`, idempotence, no-op path.
- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still pass (regression — full pytest must come back green-modulo-known-pre-existing-failures).

## Stop-lines

1. **Hype spike reveals plan-template materialization can't express hype's dynamic discovery** (or any step shape is fundamentally wrong for hype). Halt before locking the Sprint 3 schema; redesign rather than push through. Write the redesign to `docs/reshape/sprint-3/schema-redesign.md` and stop for human review.
2. **Apex contract (S1) regresses under plan mutation events**. Locked event-append + epoch CAS is what plan-mutated rides on; if multi-tab races appear, halt and fix S1's primitives, not S3's surface.
3. **Inbox path+version routing loses entries silently**. If any inbox entry vanishes (not routed, not rejected, just gone) under any supersede or tombstone path, that's a stop-line — entries must always have a deterministic destination.

## Out of scope (do NOT touch)

- The `remote-artifact` adapter implementation (Sprint 5a).
- Orchestrator porting — hype proper, event_talks, thumbnail_maker (Sprint 5a/5b). The hype spike is a throwaway port of the first three steps only.
- RunPod lift (Sprint 4).
- Anything in `astrid/packs/builtin/human_review/`, `astrid/packs/seinfeld/` — pre-existing WIP, leave untouched.
- Export / verify / cost aggregation (Sprint 5b).
- `astrid projects timeline` (the legacy reigh-app subcommand) — leave it alone in this sprint; rename happens in Sprint 5b.

## Acceptance criteria

- `git branch --show-current` returns `reshape/sprint-3`.
- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still green (modulo the 7 pre-existing unrelated failures verified in S2 — those are out of scope).
- `pytest tests/task/ tests/adapter/ tests/migrations/sprint-3/ -v` all green.
- Plan-mutation validator: every invariant has positive + negative test coverage.
- `astrid plan add-step / edit-step / remove-step / supersede-step` round-trip end-to-end on a freshly-created run.
- `astrid plan supersede-step` without `--scope` is rejected at argparse.
- `astrid plan edit-step` / `remove-step` on a dispatched step is rejected at the verb.
- `astrid plan remove-step` on an undispatched step writes a tombstone event; cursor skips it.
- `astrid claim` / `astrid unclaim` emit events; read-only session is blocked.
- `astrid ack` with neither `--agent` nor `--actor` is rejected at argparse.
- `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab, reattach, `astrid next` picks up.
- `manual` adapter: dispatch + ack-driven completion + inbox-driven completion all work.
- `remote-artifact` adapter: rejected at `astrid next` with the deferral message.
- Hype spike: `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` exists with at least 3 enumerated gaps + the schema revisions that landed in response.
- Migrations: `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_plans.py --dry-run` and `--apply` both clean against `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/`. `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_step_paths.py` likewise.

## Useful repo references

- `astrid/core/task/plan.py` — current step schema; will be rewritten.
- `astrid/core/task/events.py` — `append_event_locked` from Sprint 1; reuse for `plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`.
- `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` — current inbox; rewrite for path+version matching.
- `astrid/core/session/lease.py` — Sprint 1's lease with `writer_epoch`. CAS lives here; don't reimplement.
- `astrid/core/timeline/` — Sprint 2's package; do not modify in this sprint.
- `astrid/packs/builtin/hype/` — the spike's target; do NOT modify the real pack, only port to a scratch branch.
- `idea.md` § Sprint 3 (lines 214–295) — canonical sprint definition.
- `idea.md` § Foundation > Load-bearing decisions — the why behind these decisions.

## Style notes

- Type hints + dataclasses.
- Pathlib over os.path.
- Migration scripts: shebang, `chmod +x`, `--dry-run` default.
- All new modules include a one-line docstring.
- New tests follow existing test naming + structure conventions.
- For new event kinds (`plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`, `step_dispatched`): use `append_event_locked` from S1 — every emit is hash-chained and lease-CAS-protected.
- For supersede scope: model the three values as a string Literal (or enum), not a bare string. Argparse rejects unknown values.

## Megaplan-specific guidance

- This sprint is profile `premium/robust/high` because: (1) the kernel pieces here are downstream-cascading — every later sprint depends on these contracts; (2) the six-invariant validator is exactly the sort of contract that benefits from extra critique passes; (3) the hype spike loop has real architectural risk that wants high-depth planning.
- If the hype spike surfaces a stop-line, halt the sprint rather than push through. Document the gap and stop for human review.
- Use `astrid plan add-step` for dogfooding any sub-tasks of this sprint that are worth tracking — friction felt here is friction every user feels.

# Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — Step model overhaul, mutable plans, location-agnostic execution

## Overview

Sprint 3 is the architectural keystone of the Astrid reshape. Three things have to land in lockstep:

1. **Schema collapse** — `code`/`attested`/`nested` step kinds (`astrid/core/task/plan.py:57-86`) are replaced with a single `Step` shape that carries `adapter`, `version`, `requires_ack`, `assignee`, `produces`, `repeat`, optional `command`, optional `children`, optional `cost`, optional `superseded_by`.
2. **Mid-run mutability** — four new verbs (`plan add-step / edit-step / remove-step / supersede-step`) plus `claim`/`unclaim`, all routed through a single six-invariant validator and emitted as `plan_mutated` events on the existing Sprint 1 locked-append + epoch-CAS rails (`astrid/core/task/events.py:92`, `astrid/core/session/lease.py`).
3. **Location-agnostic execution** — three adapters (`local`, `manual`, `remote-artifact` — the last rejected at runtime) replace the implicit "command means subprocess" assumption baked into `cmd_next`/`gate.py`.

Versioned step paths (`steps/<id>/v<N>/…`) become mandatory everywhere. The inbox key becomes `(plan_step_path, step_version, item_id?)`. Migrations rewrite existing plans and step directories. A throwaway `reshape/sprint-3-hype-spike` ports the first three `builtin.hype` steps to surface schema gaps, and any revisions land back on `reshape/sprint-3` **before** the schema locks.

Branch `reshape/sprint-3` is already checked out clean — do **not** rebase or re-create. Sprint 0/1/2 contracts (locked event-append, epoch CAS, timeline package) are frozen.

The brief's four-phase approach (schema → adapters → mutation/identity verbs → tests+spike+docs) is correct *with one reordering*: the hype spike should be **opened early** (right after the schema rewrite lands in skeleton form, before adapters/mutation verbs are finalised). Idea.md is explicit that "schema revisions resulting from the spike land in Sprint 3, not deferred." If we wait until phase D to spike, we discover schema gaps after the adapter contracts and validator are already shaped around the wrong schema. Plan below builds Phase A as a *draft schema*, runs the spike against it immediately, then locks the schema before Phase B's adapters bind to it.

## Phase 1: Draft schema + path helpers (the keystone, but explicitly unlocked)

### Step 1: Audit current step model and inbox routing (`astrid/core/task/plan.py`, `astrid/core/task/inbox.py`, `astrid/core/task/gate.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** the current step union (`astrid/core/task/plan.py:17`, `:57`, `:86`) and confirm every external reference: `iter_steps_with_path` (`plan.py:155`), `step_dir_for_path` (`plan.py:115`), the `_validate_*_step` family, `_assert_unique_paths` (`plan.py:556`).
2. **Grep** every importer of `CodeStep | AttestedStep | NestedStep | TaskPlanStep` across `astrid/core/task/` and the test suite — that's the call-site surface that needs migrating.
3. **Map** the cursor's current contract (`gate.peek_current_step`, callers in `lifecycle.py`, `inbox.py:207`) so the new `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` cursor lands without behavioral drift to S1/S2 surfaces.

### Step 2: Draft the collapsed `Step` dataclass (`astrid/core/task/plan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Replace** the three step dataclasses with one frozen `Step` dataclass per idea.md § Sprint 3 spec: `id`, `version: int = 1`, `adapter: Literal["local","manual","remote-artifact"]`, `requires_ack: bool`, `assignee: str` (one of `"system" | "agent:<id>" | "any-agent" | "human:<name>" | "any-human"` — assignee parser + validator), `produces`, `repeat`, `command: str | None`, `children: tuple[Step, ...] | None`, `cost: CostEntry | None`, `superseded_by: SupersededRef | None`.
2. **Encode** the leaf-vs-group invariant in `__post_init__`: `command XOR children` (leaf has command + no children; group has children + no command).
3. **Preserve** `_assert_unique_paths`, `parse_from_ref`, and `iter_steps_with_path` (rewritten over `Step.children`).
4. **Carry forward** the `produces` dict-form validator, repeat validators, and the `_reject_orchestrators_run` guard.
5. **Mark** the schema explicitly as *draft* in a one-line module docstring until the hype spike has fed back; this is a signal to reviewers and to ourselves.

### Step 3: Versioned step paths (`astrid/core/task/plan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Update** `step_dir_for_path` (`plan.py:115`) to require a `step_version: int` parameter (default `None` for back-compat at *call sites only during this step* — flip to required once all callers are updated). Path becomes `steps/<id>/v<N>/[iterations/NNN | items/<item-id>]`.
2. **Sweep** every caller (`gate.py`, `lifecycle.py`, `lifecycle_ack.py`, `inbox.py`, `events.py`) and pass the resolved `step_version` from cursor state. Default-free at the end of the sweep.

### Step 4: Adapter ABC skeleton (`astrid/core/adapter/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `astrid/core/adapter/__init__.py` with an `Adapter` Protocol (or ABC) shaped to `cmd_next`'s existing flow:
   - `dispatch(step: Step, run_ctx: RunContext) -> DispatchResult`
   - `poll(step: Step, run_ctx: RunContext) -> PollResult`
   - `complete(step: Step, run_ctx: RunContext) -> CompleteResult`
2. **Stub** `local.py`, `manual.py`, `remote_artifact.py` with `NotImplementedError` bodies — these are placeholders the spike will exercise just enough to confirm the interface.

## Phase 2: Hype spike (open the loop early; lock the schema only after)

### Step 5: Throwaway spike branch (`reshape/sprint-3-hype-spike`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Branch** `reshape/sprint-3-hype-spike` off the current `reshape/sprint-3` HEAD. Do NOT modify `astrid/packs/builtin/hype/` on `reshape/sprint-3` — only on the spike branch.
2. **Port** the first three `builtin.hype` steps (transcribe → cut → render) to the draft `Step` shape. Manually fill `adapter`, `assignee`, `requires_ack`, `produces` per their current YAML contract.
3. **Exercise** every adapter touchpoint (local subprocess for transcribe, manual for cut decision review, etc.) against the stub `Adapter` interface — do NOT implement the adapter bodies; just confirm the interface compiles around real plan shapes.
4. **Enumerate** every gap discovered (schema field missing, adapter signature mismatch, repeat semantics, cost-field source, requires_ack vs. inbox completion, group-step `produces` aggregation, etc.) in `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` — at least 3 enumerated gaps as the acceptance criterion requires.
5. **Stop-line check**: if the spike reveals that plan-template materialization cannot express hype's dynamic discovery (i.e. mid-run inputs become mid-run new steps in a way the static-tree schema rejects), halt the sprint, write `docs/reshape/sprint-3/schema-redesign.md`, and stop for human review. This is the brief's primary architectural risk.

### Step 6: Fold spike revisions back into `reshape/sprint-3` (`astrid/core/task/plan.py`, `astrid/core/adapter/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Cherry-pick** schema field additions, adapter interface revisions, and validator-invariant clarifications discovered by the spike onto `reshape/sprint-3`.
2. **Remove** the "draft" marker docstring on `plan.py`. The schema is now locked for the rest of the sprint.
3. **Push** the spike branch for archival; do NOT merge it.

## Phase 3: Six-invariant validator + mutation verbs

### Step 7: Single-entry validator (`astrid/core/task/validator.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `astrid/core/task/validator.py` with one entry point `validate_mutation(prior: TaskPlan, proposed: TaskPlan, *, lease_epoch_actual: int, lease_epoch_expected: int) -> None` that raises a structured `MutationInvariantError(invariant_id, element, reason)` on the first failure.
2. **Implement** all six invariants against the *proposed effective tree*:
   - I1 schema (re-run `_validate_plan` on the proposed tree),
   - I2 sibling-id uniqueness at every frame (reuse `_assert_unique_paths`),
   - I3 `produces` reference integrity (no dangling refs into tombstoned/deleted steps),
   - I4 adapter validation (`adapter ∈ {local, manual, remote-artifact}`; manual must have non-empty `command`; etc.),
   - I5 `repeat.for_each.from_ref` resolves in the proposed tree,
   - I6 lease-epoch CAS (compare expected vs. actual).
3. **Surface** invariant ids as a stable enum so verbs can return non-zero with `f"validator: {err.invariant_id}: {err.reason}"`.

### Step 8: Plan-mutation verbs (`astrid/core/task/plan_verbs.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `astrid/core/task/plan_verbs.py` exporting `cmd_plan_add_step`, `cmd_plan_edit_step`, `cmd_plan_remove_step`, `cmd_plan_supersede_step`.
2. **Wire each verb** through:
   - load `plan.json` + replay `plan_mutated` events → current effective tree,
   - compute proposed tree (operation-specific),
   - `validate_mutation(prior, proposed, ...)` under writer_epoch CAS,
   - emit a single `plan_mutated` event via `append_event_locked` (`events.py:92`) carrying `{actor, writer_epoch, op, diff, timestamp}`.
3. **Enforce dispatched-step gates**:
   - `edit-step` on dispatched → reject ("use supersede-step"),
   - `remove-step` on dispatched → reject ("use abort or supersede-step"),
   - `remove-step` on undispatched → write tombstone event; cursor skips it,
   - `supersede-step` without `--scope` → **rejected at argparse** via `choices=["all","future-iterations","future-items"]` (no default; idea.md is explicit).
4. **Effective-tree replay** lives in a small helper (`apply_mutations(plan: TaskPlan, events: list[dict]) -> TaskPlan`) shared by verbs, cursor, and inbox.

### Step 9: Cursor honoring versions + tombstones + supersede scopes (`astrid/core/task/gate.py`, `astrid/core/task/lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** the cursor record to `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)`. Persist per dispatched step in the cursor state derived from events.
2. **Walk** the effective plan respecting tombstones (skip), supersede.scope=all (cursor jumps to v<N+1> iteration/item 1), scope=future-iterations (current iteration finishes against old version, iter+1 uses new), scope=future-items (current item finishes against old, next item uses new).
3. **Surface** `astrid status` to display the cursor's current step at its current version (`lifecycle.cmd_status`).

### Step 10: Inbox path+version routing (`astrid/core/task/inbox.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Rewrite** `_parse_entry` and `consume_inbox_entry` so entries match on `(plan_step_path, step_version, item_id?)`. Both fields are required on new inbox writes; legacy fields rejected (Sprint 3 schema bump).
2. **Re-route** stale entries (targeting tombstoned or fully-superseded steps) to `.rejected/<sha256>` via the existing `_move_to` pattern. **No entry may vanish silently** — every entry must land in `inbox/`, `.consumed/`, or `.rejected/`. (Stop-line.)
3. **Continue to allow** `--decision abort` to short-circuit cursor checks, but require the path+version on the entry so authorship is unambiguous.

## Phase 4: Adapters wired into `cmd_next`

### Step 11: Local adapter (`astrid/core/adapter/local.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** subprocess dispatch: spawn the `command` shell command in the project root, capture pid + start time, write a `step_dispatched` event via `append_event_locked` with `{adapter:"local", pid, command}`.
2. **Implement** completion: exit code 0 + produces checks pass → `step_completed`; non-zero or missing produces → `step_failed`. Both events include optional `cost` (typically `None` for local).
3. **Subprocess-outlives-tab**: dispatch is detach-capable; `astrid attach` + `astrid next` re-reads run state from events + inbox and resumes without needing the original parent process.

### Step 12: Manual adapter (`astrid/core/adapter/manual.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Dispatch** writes the `command` payload to a display path (the step's `v<N>/` dir as `dispatch.json`) and emits `step_dispatched`.
2. **Completion** via two paths:
   - `astrid ack` (sync, lifecycle_ack) → `step_completed`,
   - completion JSON arriving in inbox → `consume_inbox_entry` synthesizes the same event with the inbox entry's `submitted_by`.

### Step 13: Remote-artifact adapter (`astrid/core/adapter/remote_artifact.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Reject at dispatch** with the exact deferral string: `astrid start / astrid next: step '<id>' declares adapter 'remote-artifact'; not yet implemented (Sprint 5a). Use --adapter local or manual.` Exit non-zero. **The schema accepts `remote-artifact`; only runtime rejects.**

### Step 14: Wire `cmd_next` to dispatch through adapters (`astrid/core/task/lifecycle.py`, `astrid/core/task/gate.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Replace** the hard-coded subprocess assumption in `cmd_next` with adapter resolution from `step.adapter`.
2. **Emit** `step_dispatched` events through `append_event_locked` carrying `(plan_step_path, step_version, adapter, pid?)`.
3. **`requires_ack: true` enforcement**: completion-by-adapter alone is insufficient; cursor stays on the step until an `astrid ack` event arrives with non-stale `writer_epoch`.
4. **Cost**: completion events carry `cost: {amount, currency, source}` when the adapter declared one; field is optional (Sprint 4 + 5a depend on it being *reservable*).

## Phase 5: Claim verb + ack identity tightening + CLI dispatch registration

### Step 15: Claim / unclaim verbs (`astrid/core/task/claim.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `astrid/core/task/claim.py` with `cmd_claim` and `cmd_unclaim`. Argparse: `astrid claim <step> [--for human:<name>] [--for agent:<id>]`. Default `--for` resolves to the current session's identity.
2. **Block** read-only sessions (no `writer_epoch` ownership) from claiming with a clear error.
3. **Emit** `claim` / `unclaim` events via `append_event_locked` with epoch CAS.

### Step 16: Tighten `cmd_ack` argparse (`astrid/core/task/lifecycle_ack.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** argparse-level enforcement: exactly one of `--agent` / `--actor` required. `parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group(required=True)`. Neither and both both rejected at argparse with a clear message.
2. **Carry** `writer_epoch` from lease read into the synthesised event; reject stale acks.

### Step 17: Register verbs in pipeline (`astrid/pipeline.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** `LIFECYCLE_VERBS` (line 31) with `plan`, `claim`, `unclaim` (note: `plan` is a top-level verb with sub-verbs add-step / edit-step / remove-step / supersede-step).
2. **Add dispatch** branches in `_dispatch` for `plan`, `claim`, `unclaim` mirroring the `runs` / `hook` pattern (e.g. `_dispatch_plan_verbs`).
3. **Update** the help block in `_print_entrypoint_help` to document the new verbs.

## Phase 6: Migrations

### Step 18: Plan migration (`scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_plans.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_plans.py` (shebang, `chmod +x`, `--dry-run` default; `--apply` commits).
2. **Walk** `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/` (configurable root). For each `plan.json`:
   - `code` → `adapter:"local"`, `command:` preserved, `version:1`, `requires_ack:false`,
   - `attested` → `adapter:"manual"`, `command:` preserved (instructions become the dispatch payload), `version:1`, `requires_ack:true`, `assignee:` derived from `ack.kind` (`agent`/`actor` → reasonable defaults; ambiguous → TODO note in diff log),
   - `nested` → group step with `children` from the nested plan; `produces` aggregated; no `command`; `version:1`.
3. **Idempotent**: detect already-migrated plans by absence of legacy kinds and presence of `adapter`; no-op cleanly.

### Step 19: Step-path migration (`scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_step_paths.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** the script alongside the plan migration. Walk `runs/*/steps/<id>/*` and rename to `runs/*/steps/<id>/v1/*`. Iterations and items move under `v1/`.
2. **Idempotent**: skip directories that already have a `v<N>/` child.
3. **No-op path**: empty workspace exits 0 with a clear "nothing to migrate" line.

## Phase 7: Tests

### Step 20: Kernel schema + mutation tests (`tests/task/`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **`tests/task/test_plan_schema_collapsed.py`** — collapsed Step validates; leaf-vs-group XOR; each of six invariants has positive + negative case.
2. **`tests/task/test_plan_mutation_verbs.py`** — happy paths for add/edit/remove/supersede; post-dispatch edit/remove rejected; supersede without `--scope` rejected at argparse.
3. **`tests/task/test_step_versioning.py`** — supersede creates `v2/`, `v1/` survives, cursor tracks current version.
4. **`tests/task/test_cursor_supersede_scopes.py`** — all three scopes exercised under `repeat.until` and `repeat.for_each`.
5. **`tests/task/test_inbox_path_version_match.py`** — entries route across supersede; stale → `.rejected/`; no entry vanishes.
6. **`tests/task/test_claim_unclaim.py`** — claim/unclaim emit events; read-only session blocked.
7. **`tests/task/test_ack_identity_required.py`** — argparse rejects no-agent-no-actor; rejects both supplied.

### Step 21: Adapter tests (`tests/adapter/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`tests/adapter/__init__.py`** + scaffolding.
2. **`tests/adapter/test_local.py`** — subprocess dispatch, completion (exit-0 + produces), failure modes (non-zero exit, missing produces), subprocess-outlives-tab (close + reattach + next picks up).
3. **`tests/adapter/test_manual.py`** — dispatch payload landed at `v<N>/dispatch.json`; ack-driven completion; inbox-driven completion.
4. **`tests/adapter/test_remote_artifact_rejected.py`** — `astrid next` prints exact deferral string and exits non-zero.

### Step 22: Migration tests (`tests/migrations/sprint-3/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`tests/migrations/sprint-3/test_plan_migration.py`** — exercises all three old kinds collapsing to the new schema; `--dry-run` vs `--apply`; idempotence.
2. **`tests/migrations/sprint-3/test_step_path_migration.py`** — `steps/<id>/*` → `steps/<id>/v1/*`; idempotence; no-op path.

### Step 23: Rewrite or delete legacy kernel tests
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Delete or rewrite** `tests/test_task_kernel_attested.py`, `tests/test_task_kernel_nested.py`, `tests/test_task_plan_schema.py`, `tests/test_step_dir_path.py` against the collapsed schema. Brief lists these as pass-to-pass-after-rewrite.
2. **Verify** `tests/test_task_kernel_dispatch.py`, `tests/test_task_kernel_e2e.py`, `tests/test_task_kernel_events.py`, `tests/test_task_kernel_gate.py`, `tests/test_task_repeat_*` still pass after the schema swap. Adjust call-site fixtures (e.g. `CodeStep(...)` → `Step(adapter="local", command=...)`) wherever they break.
3. **Verify** Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 full suite stays green modulo the 7 known pre-existing failures from S2.

## Phase 8: Docs

### Step 24: Async-completion worked examples (`docs/reshape/sprint-3/async-completion.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** the local-subprocess-outlives-tab worked example: dispatch, detach, reattach via `astrid attach`, `astrid next` picks up where inbox left things.
2. **Write** the manual out-of-band ack worked example: completion JSON arriving in inbox, consume → `step_completed` event.

### Step 25: Hype-spike findings (`docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Document** at least 3 enumerated gaps + the specific schema revisions that landed on `reshape/sprint-3` in response (already drafted during Phase 2; finalise here).

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 1** (schema draft + path helpers + adapter ABC stub) — touches everything downstream.
2. **Phase 2** (hype spike) **early** so schema revisions land before the validator and adapters bind to the wrong shape.
3. **Phase 3** (validator + mutation verbs + cursor + inbox) — kernel locked.
4. **Phase 4** (adapters + `cmd_next` wiring + cost field) — `requires_ack` enforcement here too.
5. **Phase 5** (claim + ack tightening + pipeline.py CLI registration).
6. **Phase 6** (migrations) — once the schema is locked and verbs work end-to-end.
7. **Phase 7** (tests) — write failing specs as each component lands; the legacy-kernel-test rewrite is the final sweep.
8. **Phase 8** (docs).

## Validation Order

1. **Targeted unit tests** for each new module as it lands: `pytest tests/task/test_plan_schema_collapsed.py -v`, `pytest tests/task/test_plan_mutation_verbs.py -v`, then validator/cursor/inbox.
2. **Adapter tests**: `pytest tests/adapter/ -v`.
3. **Migration tests against a fixture tree**: `pytest tests/migrations/sprint-3/ -v` (and dry-run against `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/` as a manual smoke).
4. **Full task suite**: `pytest tests/task/ tests/adapter/ tests/migrations/sprint-3/ -v`.
5. **Regression**: `pytest` against Sprint 0/1/2 surfaces (whole repo, expecting green-modulo-known-7-S2-failures).
6. **End-to-end on a fresh run**: `astrid attach`, `astrid plan add-step / edit-step / remove-step / supersede-step` round-trip; `astrid claim` / `unclaim`; `astrid ack` argparse rejection paths; `astrid next` against a `remote-artifact` step rejection.
7. **Stop-line guard**: confirm no inbox entry vanishes (instrumentation: every consume returns `consumed | rejected | stale-left-in-inbox`).

  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "# Sprint 3 \u2014 Step model overhaul, mutable plans, location-agnostic execution\n\nAstrid reshape sprint 3. Full design: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/idea.md` \u00a7 Sprint 3 (lines 214\u2013295). This brief expands the sprint section for execution. **This is a large sprint and the architectural keystone of the reshape \u2014 premium/robust/high profile picked deliberately.**\n\nWorking directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid`. Start from branch `reshape/sprint-2`; create `reshape/sprint-3` off it and work there.\n\n## Goal\n\nCollapse the step model into one shape (no more `code`/`attested`/`nested` kinds). Make plans editable mid-run with explicit mutation rules. Make execution location-agnostic via three adapters (only `local` and `manual` ship this sprint; `remote-artifact` is reserved and rejected at runtime). Add the `claim` verb. Land the kernel-level pieces (validator, versioned step paths, mutation events, inbox path+version matching) that Sprint 4 + 5a all depend on.\n\n**No orchestrator porting (Sprint 5a/5b territory).** Sprint 3 is the kernel + the adapters; existing packs are migrated forward mechanically.\n\n## Foundational decisions (locked, do not re-debate)\n\n### The new Step shape\n\n```\nStep {\n  id: str\n  version: int                       # bumped when a dispatched step is superseded\n  adapter: \"local\" | \"manual\" | \"remote-artifact\"\n  requires_ack: bool\n  assignee: \"system\" | \"agent:<id>\" | \"any-agent\" | \"human:<name>\" | \"any-human\"\n  produces: [{name, path, check}]\n  repeat: until | for_each | none\n  command: str?                      # leaf step: the thing to run; interpreted by adapter\n  children: [Step]?                  # group step: nesting; replaces old `nested` kind\n  cost: {amount, currency, source}?  # recorded at completion\n  superseded_by: {\n    to_version: int,\n    scope: \"all\" | \"future-iterations\" | \"future-items\"  # REQUIRED; no default\n  }?\n}\n```\n\n- Leaf step has `command`, no `children`.\n- Group step has `children`, no `command`. Its `produces` aggregates from descendants.\n- `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are **gone** (migrated forward \u2014 see Migration).\n- `version` starts at 1 for the initial step; bumps to 2+ on supersede.\n\n### Supersession scope is mandatory\n\nA `supersede-step` verb without an explicit `scope` is rejected at the verb. Three scopes:\n- `\"all\"` \u2014 aborts in-flight iterations/items and restarts at iter/item 1 of the new version.\n- `\"future-iterations\"` \u2014 `repeat.until` only; current iteration finishes against old version, iter+1 uses new version.\n- `\"future-items\"` \u2014 `repeat.for_each` only; current item finishes against old version, next item uses new version.\n\n### Plan mutation rules\n\n- `plan.json` is the *initial* plan, written once at run start.\n- The *effective* plan = `plan.json` + replayed mutation events from `events.jsonl`.\n- **Append-only once dispatched.** A step is \"dispatched\" the moment `astrid next` returns its payload (event: `step_dispatched`). After dispatch:\n  - `remove-step` is rejected. Use `abort` on the run instead, or `supersede-step`.\n  - `edit-step` is rejected. Use `supersede-step` to bump version.\n  - The original `steps/<id>/v1/` directory is **never deleted**.\n- **Undispatched steps** can be added, edited, or removed freely. `remove-step` writes a tombstone event; the cursor skips tombstoned steps.\n- **Canonical step path is always versioned**: every step lives at `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`. Iterations and items live under `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/` and `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`.\n\n### The six plan-mutation invariants\n\nSingle function (call it `validate_mutation` or similar) runs **all six** invariants against the proposed effective tree:\n\n1. **Schema validation** \u2014 full re-validate of the proposed effective tree (not a diff).\n2. **Sibling-id uniqueness** at every frame (existing `plan.py:_assert_unique_paths` rule, applied to effective tree).\n3. **`produces` reference integrity** \u2014 no dangling refs to deleted/tombstoned steps.\n4. **Adapter validation** \u2014 declared adapter is `local | manual | remote-artifact`, command shape matches adapter (e.g. `manual` cannot have empty `command` string).\n5. **Repeat-source validation** \u2014 `repeat.for_each.from_ref` resolves to a real prior `produces` in the effective tree.\n6. **Lease epoch CAS** \u2014 the writer's epoch matches current (Sprint 1's lease).\n\nMutations that fail **any** check are rejected at the verb. No partial application. The verb returns a non-zero exit with the specific invariant that failed.\n\n### Three adapters; only two ship this sprint\n\n- **`local`** \u2014 `command` is a shell command run as a subprocess in the project root. Outputs land in `produces/`. Completion = exit code 0 + produces checks pass.\n- **`manual`** \u2014 agent (or human) runs the work out-of-band; `command` is the dispatch payload (instructions). The agent is responsible for landing outputs in `produces/`. Completion = `astrid ack` (sync) or completion JSON in inbox (async).\n- **`remote-artifact`** \u2014 schema-reserved here, **rejected at runtime** in Sprint 3 with: `astrid start / astrid next: step '<id>' declares adapter 'remote-artifact'; not yet implemented (Sprint 5a). Use --adapter local or manual.` Full implementation lands in Sprint 5a alongside the hype port.\n\nAdapters are first-class code, not config.\n\n### Ack identity at ack time, not author time\n\n`astrid ack <step> --decision {approve,retry,iterate,abort} --agent <id>|--actor <name> [--evidence <path>]`. Both `--agent` and `--actor` are required (one or the other) \u2014 **no anonymous acks**. Every ack carries the `writer_epoch` it read; stale acks are rejected by the kernel.\n\n### Authoring split\n\n- **Leaf-template** \u2014 what we used to call an executor. One concrete unit of work. Same pack layout on disk (`pack/<name>/run.py` + `executor.yaml`).\n- **Plan-template** \u2014 what we used to call an orchestrator: a reusable composition. Materializes into a step subtree at plan-build or plan-mutation time.\n\nRuntime never sees templates \u2014 only the resulting step tree. During Sprint 3, the new template kinds land **alongside** the legacy `OrchestratorDefinition` YAML \u2014 both readable. Legacy YAML is materialized into the new step tree on the way in. (Sprint 5b removes legacy YAML acceptance.)\n\n### Capture-everything rule\n\nAnything producing an audit-worthy artifact = a step. The friction is small: `astrid plan add-step --command '...' --produces '...'` + `astrid next`. The alternative \u2014 agent runs python ad-hoc, artifact appears with no provenance \u2014 is what we're eliminating. Truly ephemeral commands don't need it.\n\n## Deliverables\n\n### Step schema + plan rewrite\n\n- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/plan.py` against the new step shape. Keep `_assert_unique_paths` (or its equivalent in the new code) and the validators for the existing invariants; add what's needed for the new ones.\n- Versioned step path everywhere: `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`. Update all path helpers in `astrid/core/task/`.\n- **`code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are gone.** Migration script (see below) rewrites existing plans.\n\n### Plan-mutation verbs + event\n\n- `astrid plan add-step` \u2014 insert a step into the effective tree at a given path. Position via `--after <step-path>` / `--before <step-path>` / `--into <group-path>`.\n- `astrid plan edit-step` \u2014 modify an undispatched step (fields specifiable as flags).\n- `astrid plan remove-step` \u2014 write a tombstone event for an undispatched step. Rejected on dispatched steps.\n- `astrid plan supersede-step` \u2014 bump version on a dispatched (or any) step. **Requires `--scope {all,future-iterations,future-items}`.**\n- All four verbs emit a single `plan_mutated` event in `events.jsonl` carrying: actor, writer_epoch, diff (the specific operation), timestamp. The hash chain extends to plan mutations.\n\n### Plan-mutation validator\n\n- Single entry point that runs all six invariants. Every verb routes through it. Rejection is total.\n- Returns structured error: which invariant failed + the specific element that triggered it.\n- Test: each invariant has at least one positive (passes) and one negative (rejects with the right error code) case.\n\n### Cursor\n\n- Cursor stores `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` per dispatched step.\n- Walks the effective plan respecting tombstones and supersedes (honoring `scope` per repeat semantics).\n- `astrid status` shows the cursor's current step at its current version.\n\n### Inbox path+version match\n\n- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` so entries match on `(plan_step_path, step_version, item_id?)` not just the leaf step id.\n- Inbox entries from before a supersede event route to the original version's directory; entries after route to the new version.\n- Stale entries that target a tombstoned or fully-superseded step move to `.rejected/` (existing pattern \u2014 extend it).\n\n### Adapters\n\n- `astrid/core/adapter/local.py` \u2014 subprocess-based local execution. Dispatch = spawn subprocess (sync or detached); completion = exit code 0 + produces checks; failure = non-zero exit OR missing produces.\n- `astrid/core/adapter/manual.py` \u2014 emit dispatch payload (`command` field) to the agent's display path; completion = `astrid ack` event OR completion JSON arriving in inbox.\n- `astrid/core/adapter/remote_artifact.py` \u2014 stub that rejects at dispatch time with the deferral message above.\n- All three implement a common interface (e.g. `Adapter.dispatch(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.poll(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.complete(step, run_ctx)`). Pick the interface to fit how `astrid next` already works.\n\n### Claim verb\n\n- `astrid claim <step> [--for human:<name>] [--for agent:<id>]`. Default `--for` = current session's identity.\n- Emits `claim` / `unclaim` events.\n- Read-only sessions blocked from claiming.\n\n### `requires_ack` enforcement\n\n- Steps with `requires_ack: true` cannot complete on adapter completion alone; require an `astrid ack` event with a non-stale `writer_epoch`.\n- `--agent` or `--actor` is **required** at ack time. Argparse rejects neither/both.\n\n### Mutating verbs include `writer_epoch` + CAS\n\n- Every mutating verb reads the current lease's `writer_epoch` before mutating; bumps it as part of the mutation; rejects if the epoch advanced under it.\n- Relies on Sprint 1's lease primitives directly \u2014 do NOT reimplement.\n\n### Cost field on completion events\n\n- Each step completion event (`step_completed`, `step_failed`) carries `cost: {amount, currency, source}` if the adapter or executing code declared one.\n- Adapters MAY emit cost; local subprocess execution typically won't. The field is optional but the schema reserves it (Sprint 4 + 5a depend on this).\n\n### Inbox-driven async-completion documented + exercised\n\n- Worked example for `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab. Demonstrate that closing the tab, opening a new one, `astrid attach`, `astrid next` picks up wherever inbox left things.\n- Worked example for `manual` adapter: out-of-band ack landing as a completion JSON in inbox.\n- Documentation lives at `docs/reshape/sprint-3/async-completion.md`.\n\n### Migrations\n\n- **Plan migration** \u2014 every existing `plan.json` rewritten to the collapsed schema. Every existing step gets `adapter: local` and `version: 1`. `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds rewritten to the new shape (best-effort mapping; ambiguous cases get `adapter: local` + a TODO note in the diff log).\n- **Step path migration** \u2014 every existing `steps/<id>/...` directory becomes `steps/<id>/v1/...` in one shot. Run on `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/` (which is currently mostly empty \u2014 but the script must be idempotent and exercise the no-op path cleanly).\n- Both migrations live under `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/`. `--dry-run` default; `--apply` commits.\n\n### Hype port spike \u2014 back half of sprint, throwaway, do NOT merge to reshape/sprint-3\n\n- Scratch branch `reshape/sprint-3-hype-spike` off `reshape/sprint-3`.\n- Take the first three steps of `builtin.hype` only (transcribe \u2192 cut \u2192 render) and port them against the new step model.\n- Deliverable is `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` enumerating every schema gap, adapter mismatch, and cost-field ambiguity discovered.\n- **Schema revisions resulting from the spike land in Sprint 3, not deferred to a follow-up.** Revise the schema while it's still cheap.\n- The spike branch is throwaway \u2014 push it for archival but do not merge.\n\n### Tests\n\n- `tests/task/test_plan_schema_collapsed.py` \u2014 new step schema validation, including all six invariants positive + negative.\n- `tests/task/test_plan_mutation_verbs.py` \u2014 add/edit/remove/supersede each succeed in the happy path and reject correctly when post-dispatch / scope-missing / invariant-failing.\n- `tests/task/test_step_versioning.py` \u2014 supersede produces `v2/`, `v1/` survives, cursor tracks correct version.\n- `tests/task/test_cursor_supersede_scopes.py` \u2014 all three scopes exercised under repeat.until and repeat.for_each.\n- `tests/task/test_inbox_path_version_match.py` \u2014 entries route correctly across a supersede event; stale entries land in `.rejected/`.\n- `tests/adapter/test_local.py` \u2014 subprocess dispatch, completion, failure modes.\n- `tests/adapter/test_manual.py` \u2014 dispatch payload emission, ack-driven completion, inbox-driven completion.\n- `tests/adapter/test_remote_artifact_rejected.py` \u2014 `astrid next` rejects cleanly with the deferral message.\n- `tests/task/test_claim_unclaim.py` \u2014 claim emits event, unclaim emits event, read-only session blocked.\n- `tests/task/test_ack_identity_required.py` \u2014 argparse rejects no-agent-no-actor; both rejected too.\n- `tests/migrations/test_plan_migration.py` \u2014 collapsed-schema rewrite across all old step kinds.\n- `tests/migrations/test_step_path_migration.py` \u2014 `steps/<id>/...` \u2192 `steps/<id>/v1/...`, idempotence, no-op path.\n- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still pass (regression \u2014 full pytest must come back green-modulo-known-pre-existing-failures).\n\n## Stop-lines\n\n1. **Hype spike reveals plan-template materialization can't express hype's dynamic discovery** (or any step shape is fundamentally wrong for hype). Halt before locking the Sprint 3 schema; redesign rather than push through. Write the redesign to `docs/reshape/sprint-3/schema-redesign.md` and stop for human review.\n2. **Apex contract (S1) regresses under plan mutation events**. Locked event-append + epoch CAS is what plan-mutated rides on; if multi-tab races appear, halt and fix S1's primitives, not S3's surface.\n3. **Inbox path+version routing loses entries silently**. If any inbox entry vanishes (not routed, not rejected, just gone) under any supersede or tombstone path, that's a stop-line \u2014 entries must always have a deterministic destination.\n\n## Out of scope (do NOT touch)\n\n- The `remote-artifact` adapter implementation (Sprint 5a).\n- Orchestrator porting \u2014 hype proper, event_talks, thumbnail_maker (Sprint 5a/5b). The hype spike is a throwaway port of the first three steps only.\n- RunPod lift (Sprint 4).\n- Anything in `astrid/packs/builtin/human_review/`, `astrid/packs/seinfeld/` \u2014 pre-existing WIP, leave untouched.\n- Export / verify / cost aggregation (Sprint 5b).\n- `astrid projects timeline` (the legacy reigh-app subcommand) \u2014 leave it alone in this sprint; rename happens in Sprint 5b.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- `git branch --show-current` returns `reshape/sprint-3`.\n- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still green (modulo the 7 pre-existing unrelated failures verified in S2 \u2014 those are out of scope).\n- `pytest tests/task/ tests/adapter/ tests/migrations/sprint-3/ -v` all green.\n- Plan-mutation validator: every invariant has positive + negative test coverage.\n- `astrid plan add-step / edit-step / remove-step / supersede-step` round-trip end-to-end on a freshly-created run.\n- `astrid plan supersede-step` without `--scope` is rejected at argparse.\n- `astrid plan edit-step` / `remove-step` on a dispatched step is rejected at the verb.\n- `astrid plan remove-step` on an undispatched step writes a tombstone event; cursor skips it.\n- `astrid claim` / `astrid unclaim` emit events; read-only session is blocked.\n- `astrid ack` with neither `--agent` nor `--actor` is rejected at argparse.\n- `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab, reattach, `astrid next` picks up.\n- `manual` adapter: dispatch + ack-driven completion + inbox-driven completion all work.\n- `remote-artifact` adapter: rejected at `astrid next` with the deferral message.\n- Hype spike: `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` exists with at least 3 enumerated gaps + the schema revisions that landed in response.\n- Migrations: `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_plans.py --dry-run` and `--apply` both clean against `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/`. `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_step_paths.py` likewise.\n\n## Useful repo references\n\n- `astrid/core/task/plan.py` \u2014 current step schema; will be rewritten.\n- `astrid/core/task/events.py` \u2014 `append_event_locked` from Sprint 1; reuse for `plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`.\n- `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` \u2014 current inbox; rewrite for path+version matching.\n- `astrid/core/session/lease.py` \u2014 Sprint 1's lease with `writer_epoch`. Argparse rejects unknown values.\n\n## Megaplan-specific guidance\n\n- This sprint is profile `premium/robust/high` because: (1) the kernel pieces here are downstream-cascading \u2014 every later sprint depends on these contracts; (2) the six-invariant validator is exactly the sort of contract that benefits from extra critique passes; (3) the hype spike loop has real architectural risk that wants high-depth planning.\n- If the hype spike surfaces a stop-line, halt the sprint rather than push through.         Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "append-path-integrity": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "removing verify_chain at gate.py:533 means mid-chain corruption only caught by offline audit.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-sessions"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-005",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked v5's worker auth story against `/users/user_c042661f/documents/banodoco-workspace/banodoco-worker/worker_jwt.py` and `worker.py`. `worker_jwt.verify_user_jwt` validates signature/audience/expiry and returns the jwt subject; it does not prove project or timeline ownership. the reference worker separately calls `_verify_project_ownership(rpc, project_id, verified.user_id)` via a service-role read of `projects.user_id` before writing. v5 says `worker_jwt.py` confirms the user owns the timeline and only tests missing ownership claims, but it does not add the separate project-ownership check, which is required when the terminal write uses service-role.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "wire-artagents-into-reigh-app-20260504-1033"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 2.4 token policy: posts and get /state.json require token, but static `--serve prefix=dir` gets are unauthenticated 'so <video> tags work without js-injected headers'. for seinfeld clips this is benign, but builtin.human_review is generic \u2014 a future caller mounting sensitive content (e.g. internal training stills, eval-grid renders) would expose it to any localhost process. mitigation: token-protect static gets too and have the page set a fetch cookie, or document the unauthenticated-static-content caveat in stage.md so callers know not to mount secrets.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "seinfeld-dataset-medium"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-020",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan swaps `_validate_default_timeline_id` in `astrid/core/project/schema.py` from `validate_project_slug` to `validate_timeline_ulid` (step 7), but the existing error message on line 223 still reads 'project.default_timeline_id must be a slug string or null'. after the validator change, this user-facing string becomes factually incorrect because the field will hold a ulid, not a slug. the plan does not mention updating this message, which will mislead operators when validation fails.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-timelines"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-021",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan adds `timelines ls` to `_verb_is_unbound_allowlisted` in `pipeline.py` (step 12) while simultaneously requiring every timeline handler, including `cmd_ls`, to gate behind a bound session (step 11). since `timelines ls` operates on the 'current project' per the brief, it needs a session to resolve the project slug. the pipeline allowlist entry is therefore contradictory: the handler will reject the call even though the pipeline permitted it. the plan should either remove the allowlist entry or explicitly design `cmd_ls` to accept a `--project` override for unbound invocation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-timelines"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "auth-scope": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-006",
      "concern": "auth scope: v5 broadens service-role timeline writes from the worker-only path into generic supabasedataprovider, open_in_reigh, and project cli edit flows, conflicting with sd-009's user-jwt/pat ownership-bound cli model.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "wire-artagents-into-reigh-app-20260504-1033"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cli-allowlist": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "gating astrid doctor requires session-bound state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-sessions"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked v5 against sd-009 and the corrected user notes. the corrected facts only establish the service-role rpc path for the trusted aa worker, but v5 makes the generic `supabasedataprovider.save_timeline`, `open_in_reigh` default flow, and project cli edit verbs all take `service_role_key` and call the versioned rpc. that broadens service-role use beyond the worker and conflicts with the brief's user-jwt/pat ownership-bound cli path.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "wire-artagents-into-reigh-app-20260504-1033"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: brief acceptance criterion #1 names the output file `human_review.final.json`, but step 7.4 introduces per-round filenames `topup-round-n.final.json` and step 8.3 writes a union into the top-level `manifest.json`. plan does not explicitly state whether round-0 (the initial review) writes to `human_review.final.json` to match the brief's literal filename, only that the merged manifest is correct. minor naming inconsistency that could surprise anyone scripting against the brief's filename.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "seinfeld-dataset-medium"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked the step 1 source-reference paths for the local packages. the package manifests live at the package roots, but the source files are under `typescript/src/index.ts`; `packages/timeline-ops/src/index.ts` and `packages/timeline-schema/src/index.ts` do not exist. v5 says to read `../banodoco-workspace/packages/timeline-ops/{package.json,src/index.ts}` and the matching schema path, so that documentation step still points at missing source files even though the install targets are fixed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "wire-artagents-into-reigh-app-20260504-1033"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-022",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: `astrid/core/session/cli.py` `cmd_attach` line 206 prints `timeline: {args.timeline or none_placeholder}` to stdout. when `--timeline` is omitted and a project default is resolved, `args.timeline` remains `none`, so the stdout print will still display `(none)` even though a default timeline was bound. step 10 specifies the stderr hint (`using default timeline: <slug>...`) but does not explicitly update the stdout print line to use the resolved slug. this creates a user-facing discrepancy where stdout says `(none)` but stderr says a default was used.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-timelines"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-023",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: `astrid/core/session/cli.py` `cmd_sessions_ls` at line 225 displays `timeline={s.timeline or none_placeholder}` for every session. the plan adds `timeline_id` to the `session` dataclass but does not update `cmd_sessions_ls` to resolve a slug from `timeline_id` when `timeline` is absent. in normal operation `cmd_attach` populates both fields, but session files resumed from older versions or edge cases could show `(none)` despite having a bound timeline ulid.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-timelines"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-004",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the reference worker call chain for write authorization. in `banodoco-worker/worker.py`, after jwt verification the caller creates `supabasetimelinerpc(audited_user_id=verified.user_id)` and then calls `_verify_project_ownership(rpc, project_id, verified.user_id)` before the service-role write. v5's worker caller path lacks that explicit ownership-verification call and relies on `worker_jwt.py` to prove ownership, which the reference `worker_jwt.py` does not do.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "wire-artagents-into-reigh-app-20260504-1033"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-012",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: edge case not covered: in `--review-only` mode where a `--state` file already exists with completed decisions from a previous full review, get /state.json will return them and the reviewer will load + auto-cursor past every clip. plan doesn't address whether the user can force a fresh review or whether re-running --review-only intentionally is idempotent (just re-submits prior decisions). workflow ambiguity worth nailing down.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "seinfeld-dataset-medium"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-024",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the plan adds `timeline_id` to `lease.json` by modifying the `write_lease_init` call site at `lifecycle.py:237`, but it never explicitly lists updating the `write_lease_init` function signature in `astrid/core/session/lease.py`. more importantly, `lease.py` contains three other lease-mutation functions (`bump_epoch_and_swap_session`, `claim_orphan_lease`, `release_writer_lease`) that construct lease dicts explicitly with only the canonical keys `writer_epoch`, `attached_session_id`, and `plan_hash`. any session takeover, orphan claim, or lease release will silently strip `timeline_id` from the lease. the plan must either update all four lease-mutation sites to preserve passthrough fields or explicitly carry `timeline_id` forward, or downstream code that reads `timeline_id` from the lease will see an intermittent `none` after a takeover.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-timelines"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-025",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `cmd_attach` has two branches: new-session (line 190-200) and resume-session (line 153-164). the plan step 10 only describes populating `session.timeline_id` in the new-session branch. when `--session <id>` is passed, the existing session file is loaded as-is via `session.from_dict`; old sprint 1 sessions lack `timeline_id`, so it remains `none`. while `session.from_dict` handles missing keys gracefully via `_optional_str`, this creates a transition-period gap where resumed sessions have no ulid identity. internal callers that later assume `session.timeline_id` is always populated (e.g., sprint 5a orchestrator/executor wiring) will need defensive none checks or the resume branch should re-resolve the project's default timeline to backfill the field.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-timelines"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "gate-command-signature": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-017",
      "concern": "gate_command writer_epoch kwarg is redundant under v4's writercontext-at-entry model.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-sessions"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "migration-ux": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-016",
      "concern": "migration dry-run vs --apply behavior on malformed active_run.json not explicitly distinguished.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-sessions"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "package-source-path-references": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "package source path references: v5 fixes package-root file dependencies, but phase 0 step 1 still names package-root `src/index.ts` files that do not exist; the actual source files are under `typescript/src/index.ts`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "wire-artagents-into-reigh-app-20260504-1033"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: checked write paths outside the worker in v5. `open_in_reigh` and `projects edit` now route through `supabasedataprovider.save_timeline(..., service_role_key=...)`, while the epic still says cli verbs talk via `reigh-data-fetch` plus `timeline-import` and sd-009 says user jwt for ownership-bound cli calls with service-role only for the worker. the worker correction is valid, but applying it to every authoring path is broader than the corrected facts justify.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "wire-artagents-into-reigh-app-20260504-1033"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: custom range parsing is being reimplemented in human_review (step 2.2) rather than refactored out of render/run.py for shared use. two copies of range-handling logic is acceptable scope per the brief's 'don't pad with abstractions' guidance, but worth noting as duplication that may rot independently if the render handler is fixed for a bug.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "seinfeld-dataset-medium"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-026",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the migration script (step 17) treats every existing `runs/<run-id>/timeline.json` as a legacy assembly to rewrite into the new `timelines/<ulid>/` shape. however, `mirror_hype_artifacts` in `astrid/core/project/run.py` (line 250) copies `hype.timeline.json` \u2192 `timeline.json` into run directories during orchestrator/executor run finalization. after s2, newly finalized runs will contain `timeline.json` files at the same path that are hype render artifacts (tracks/clips schema), not legacy assemblies. the migration script does not validate json shape before processing, so re-running it or running against a workspace with already-finalized runs could misread hype artifacts as assemblies, causing data corruption or crashes.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-timelines"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "takeover-epoch-cas": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "bump_epoch_and_swap_session must pass new_epoch (n+1) to internal append_event_locked.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-sessions"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "threads-scope-contraction": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "pack-level --thread coexists with removed `astrid thread` cli \u2014 two thread vocabularies.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-sessions"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "worker-authorization": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "worker authorization: v5 says jwks verification confirms timeline ownership, but the reference `worker_jwt.py` only verifies jwt identity; the reference worker separately performs a service-role project ownership read before the service-role rpc.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "wire-artagents-into-reigh-app-20260504-1033"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "writer-auth-test-ergonomics": [

      "concern": "legacy append_event wrapper + missing lease raises notwritererror; tests must seed lease.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-sessions"
      ]
    }
  ]
}

        Robustness level:
        robust

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
