        Idea:
# Sprint 3 — Step model overhaul, mutable plans, location-agnostic execution

Astrid reshape sprint 3. Full design: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/idea.md` § Sprint 3 (lines 214–295). This brief expands the sprint section for execution. **This is a large sprint and the architectural keystone of the reshape — premium/robust/high profile picked deliberately.**

Working directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid`. Start from branch `reshape/sprint-2`; create `reshape/sprint-3` off it and work there.

## Goal

Collapse the step model into one shape (no more `code`/`attested`/`nested` kinds). Make plans editable mid-run with explicit mutation rules. Make execution location-agnostic via three adapters (only `local` and `manual` ship this sprint; `remote-artifact` is reserved and rejected at runtime). Add the `claim` verb. Land the kernel-level pieces (validator, versioned step paths, mutation events, inbox path+version matching) that Sprint 4 + 5a all depend on.

**No orchestrator porting (Sprint 5a/5b territory).** Sprint 3 is the kernel + the adapters; existing packs are migrated forward mechanically.

## Foundational decisions (locked, do not re-debate)

### The new Step shape

```
Step {
  id: str
  version: int                       # bumped when a dispatched step is superseded
  adapter: "local" | "manual" | "remote-artifact"
  requires_ack: bool
  assignee: "system" | "agent:<id>" | "any-agent" | "human:<name>" | "any-human"
  produces: [{name, path, check}]
  repeat: until | for_each | none
  command: str?                      # leaf step: the thing to run; interpreted by adapter
  children: [Step]?                  # group step: nesting; replaces old `nested` kind
  cost: {amount, currency, source}?  # recorded at completion
  superseded_by: {
    to_version: int,
    scope: "all" | "future-iterations" | "future-items"  # REQUIRED; no default
  }?
}
```

- Leaf step has `command`, no `children`.
- Group step has `children`, no `command`. Its `produces` aggregates from descendants.
- `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are **gone** (migrated forward — see Migration).
- `version` starts at 1 for the initial step; bumps to 2+ on supersede.

### Supersession scope is mandatory

A `supersede-step` verb without an explicit `scope` is rejected at the verb. Three scopes:
- `"all"` — aborts in-flight iterations/items and restarts at iter/item 1 of the new version.
- `"future-iterations"` — `repeat.until` only; current iteration finishes against old version, iter+1 uses new version.
- `"future-items"` — `repeat.for_each` only; current item finishes against old version, next item uses new version.

### Plan mutation rules

- `plan.json` is the *initial* plan, written once at run start.
- The *effective* plan = `plan.json` + replayed mutation events from `events.jsonl`.
- **Append-only once dispatched.** A step is "dispatched" the moment `astrid next` returns its payload (event: `step_dispatched`). After dispatch:
  - `remove-step` is rejected. Use `abort` on the run instead, or `supersede-step`.
  - `edit-step` is rejected. Use `supersede-step` to bump version.
  - The original `steps/<id>/v1/` directory is **never deleted**.
- **Undispatched steps** can be added, edited, or removed freely. `remove-step` writes a tombstone event; the cursor skips tombstoned steps.
- **Canonical step path is always versioned**: every step lives at `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`. Iterations and items live under `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/` and `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`.

### The six plan-mutation invariants

Single function (call it `validate_mutation` or similar) runs **all six** invariants against the proposed effective tree:

1. **Schema validation** — full re-validate of the proposed effective tree (not a diff).
2. **Sibling-id uniqueness** at every frame (existing `plan.py:_assert_unique_paths` rule, applied to effective tree).
3. **`produces` reference integrity** — no dangling refs to deleted/tombstoned steps.
4. **Adapter validation** — declared adapter is `local | manual | remote-artifact`, command shape matches adapter (e.g. `manual` cannot have empty `command` string).
5. **Repeat-source validation** — `repeat.for_each.from_ref` resolves to a real prior `produces` in the effective tree.
6. **Lease epoch CAS** — the writer's epoch matches current (Sprint 1's lease).

Mutations that fail **any** check are rejected at the verb. No partial application. The verb returns a non-zero exit with the specific invariant that failed.

### Three adapters; only two ship this sprint

- **`local`** — `command` is a shell command run as a subprocess in the project root. Outputs land in `produces/`. Completion = exit code 0 + produces checks pass.
- **`manual`** — agent (or human) runs the work out-of-band; `command` is the dispatch payload (instructions). The agent is responsible for landing outputs in `produces/`. Completion = `astrid ack` (sync) or completion JSON in inbox (async).
- **`remote-artifact`** — schema-reserved here, **rejected at runtime** in Sprint 3 with: `astrid start / astrid next: step '<id>' declares adapter 'remote-artifact'; not yet implemented (Sprint 5a). Use --adapter local or manual.` Full implementation lands in Sprint 5a alongside the hype port.

Adapters are first-class code, not config.

### Ack identity at ack time, not author time

`astrid ack <step> --decision {approve,retry,iterate,abort} --agent <id>|--actor <name> [--evidence <path>]`. Both `--agent` and `--actor` are required (one or the other) — **no anonymous acks**. Every ack carries the `writer_epoch` it read; stale acks are rejected by the kernel.

### Authoring split

- **Leaf-template** — what we used to call an executor. One concrete unit of work. Same pack layout on disk (`pack/<name>/run.py` + `executor.yaml`).
- **Plan-template** — what we used to call an orchestrator: a reusable composition. Materializes into a step subtree at plan-build or plan-mutation time.

Runtime never sees templates — only the resulting step tree. During Sprint 3, the new template kinds land **alongside** the legacy `OrchestratorDefinition` YAML — both readable. Legacy YAML is materialized into the new step tree on the way in. (Sprint 5b removes legacy YAML acceptance.)

### Capture-everything rule

Anything producing an audit-worthy artifact = a step. The friction is small: `astrid plan add-step --command '...' --produces '...'` + `astrid next`. The alternative — agent runs python ad-hoc, artifact appears with no provenance — is what we're eliminating. Truly ephemeral commands don't need it.

## Deliverables

### Step schema + plan rewrite

- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/plan.py` against the new step shape. Keep `_assert_unique_paths` (or its equivalent in the new code) and the validators for the existing invariants; add what's needed for the new ones.
- Versioned step path everywhere: `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`. Update all path helpers in `astrid/core/task/`.
- **`code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are gone.** Migration script (see below) rewrites existing plans.

### Plan-mutation verbs + event

- `astrid plan add-step` — insert a step into the effective tree at a given path. Position via `--after <step-path>` / `--before <step-path>` / `--into <group-path>`.
- `astrid plan edit-step` — modify an undispatched step (fields specifiable as flags).
- `astrid plan remove-step` — write a tombstone event for an undispatched step. Rejected on dispatched steps.
- `astrid plan supersede-step` — bump version on a dispatched (or any) step. **Requires `--scope {all,future-iterations,future-items}`.**
- All four verbs emit a single `plan_mutated` event in `events.jsonl` carrying: actor, writer_epoch, diff (the specific operation), timestamp. The hash chain extends to plan mutations.

### Plan-mutation validator

- Single entry point that runs all six invariants. Every verb routes through it. Rejection is total.
- Returns structured error: which invariant failed + the specific element that triggered it.
- Test: each invariant has at least one positive (passes) and one negative (rejects with the right error code) case.

### Cursor

- Cursor stores `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` per dispatched step.
- Walks the effective plan respecting tombstones and supersedes (honoring `scope` per repeat semantics).
- `astrid status` shows the cursor's current step at its current version.

### Inbox path+version match

- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` so entries match on `(plan_step_path, step_version, item_id?)` not just the leaf step id.
- Inbox entries from before a supersede event route to the original version's directory; entries after route to the new version.
- Stale entries that target a tombstoned or fully-superseded step move to `.rejected/` (existing pattern — extend it).

### Adapters

- `astrid/core/adapter/local.py` — subprocess-based local execution. Dispatch = spawn subprocess (sync or detached); completion = exit code 0 + produces checks; failure = non-zero exit OR missing produces.
- `astrid/core/adapter/manual.py` — emit dispatch payload (`command` field) to the agent's display path; completion = `astrid ack` event OR completion JSON arriving in inbox.
- `astrid/core/adapter/remote_artifact.py` — stub that rejects at dispatch time with the deferral message above.
- All three implement a common interface (e.g. `Adapter.dispatch(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.poll(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.complete(step, run_ctx)`). Pick the interface to fit how `astrid next` already works.

### Claim verb

- `astrid claim <step> [--for human:<name>] [--for agent:<id>]`. Default `--for` = current session's identity.
- Emits `claim` / `unclaim` events.
- Read-only sessions blocked from claiming.

### `requires_ack` enforcement

- Steps with `requires_ack: true` cannot complete on adapter completion alone; require an `astrid ack` event with a non-stale `writer_epoch`.
- `--agent` or `--actor` is **required** at ack time. Argparse rejects neither/both.

### Mutating verbs include `writer_epoch` + CAS

- Every mutating verb reads the current lease's `writer_epoch` before mutating; bumps it as part of the mutation; rejects if the epoch advanced under it.
- Relies on Sprint 1's lease primitives directly — do NOT reimplement.

### Cost field on completion events

- Each step completion event (`step_completed`, `step_failed`) carries `cost: {amount, currency, source}` if the adapter or executing code declared one.
- Adapters MAY emit cost; local subprocess execution typically won't. The field is optional but the schema reserves it (Sprint 4 + 5a depend on this).

### Inbox-driven async-completion documented + exercised

- Worked example for `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab. Demonstrate that closing the tab, opening a new one, `astrid attach`, `astrid next` picks up wherever inbox left things.
- Worked example for `manual` adapter: out-of-band ack landing as a completion JSON in inbox.
- Documentation lives at `docs/reshape/sprint-3/async-completion.md`.

### Migrations

- **Plan migration** — every existing `plan.json` rewritten to the collapsed schema. Every existing step gets `adapter: local` and `version: 1`. `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds rewritten to the new shape (best-effort mapping; ambiguous cases get `adapter: local` + a TODO note in the diff log).
- **Step path migration** — every existing `steps/<id>/...` directory becomes `steps/<id>/v1/...` in one shot. Run on `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/` (which is currently mostly empty — but the script must be idempotent and exercise the no-op path cleanly).
- Both migrations live under `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/`. `--dry-run` default; `--apply` commits.

### Hype port spike — back half of sprint, throwaway, do NOT merge to reshape/sprint-3

- Scratch branch `reshape/sprint-3-hype-spike` off `reshape/sprint-3`.
- Take the first three steps of `builtin.hype` only (transcribe → cut → render) and port them against the new step model.
- Deliverable is `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` enumerating every schema gap, adapter mismatch, and cost-field ambiguity discovered.
- **Schema revisions resulting from the spike land in Sprint 3, not deferred to a follow-up.** Revise the schema while it's still cheap.
- The spike branch is throwaway — push it for archival but do not merge.

### Tests

- `tests/task/test_plan_schema_collapsed.py` — new step schema validation, including all six invariants positive + negative.
- `tests/task/test_plan_mutation_verbs.py` — add/edit/remove/supersede each succeed in the happy path and reject correctly when post-dispatch / scope-missing / invariant-failing.
- `tests/task/test_step_versioning.py` — supersede produces `v2/`, `v1/` survives, cursor tracks correct version.
- `tests/task/test_cursor_supersede_scopes.py` — all three scopes exercised under repeat.until and repeat.for_each.
- `tests/task/test_inbox_path_version_match.py` — entries route correctly across a supersede event; stale entries land in `.rejected/`.
- `tests/adapter/test_local.py` — subprocess dispatch, completion, failure modes.
- `tests/adapter/test_manual.py` — dispatch payload emission, ack-driven completion, inbox-driven completion.
- `tests/adapter/test_remote_artifact_rejected.py` — `astrid next` rejects cleanly with the deferral message.
- `tests/task/test_claim_unclaim.py` — claim emits event, unclaim emits event, read-only session blocked.
- `tests/task/test_ack_identity_required.py` — argparse rejects no-agent-no-actor; both rejected too.
- `tests/migrations/test_plan_migration.py` — collapsed-schema rewrite across all old step kinds.
- `tests/migrations/test_step_path_migration.py` — `steps/<id>/...` → `steps/<id>/v1/...`, idempotence, no-op path.
- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still pass (regression — full pytest must come back green-modulo-known-pre-existing-failures).

## Stop-lines

1. **Hype spike reveals plan-template materialization can't express hype's dynamic discovery** (or any step shape is fundamentally wrong for hype). Halt before locking the Sprint 3 schema; redesign rather than push through. Write the redesign to `docs/reshape/sprint-3/schema-redesign.md` and stop for human review.
2. **Apex contract (S1) regresses under plan mutation events**. Locked event-append + epoch CAS is what plan-mutated rides on; if multi-tab races appear, halt and fix S1's primitives, not S3's surface.
3. **Inbox path+version routing loses entries silently**. If any inbox entry vanishes (not routed, not rejected, just gone) under any supersede or tombstone path, that's a stop-line — entries must always have a deterministic destination.

## Out of scope (do NOT touch)

- The `remote-artifact` adapter implementation (Sprint 5a).
- Orchestrator porting — hype proper, event_talks, thumbnail_maker (Sprint 5a/5b). The hype spike is a throwaway port of the first three steps only.
- RunPod lift (Sprint 4).
- Anything in `astrid/packs/builtin/human_review/`, `astrid/packs/seinfeld/` — pre-existing WIP, leave untouched.
- Export / verify / cost aggregation (Sprint 5b).
- `astrid projects timeline` (the legacy reigh-app subcommand) — leave it alone in this sprint; rename happens in Sprint 5b.

## Acceptance criteria

- `git branch --show-current` returns `reshape/sprint-3`.
- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still green (modulo the 7 pre-existing unrelated failures verified in S2 — those are out of scope).
- `pytest tests/task/ tests/adapter/ tests/migrations/sprint-3/ -v` all green.
- Plan-mutation validator: every invariant has positive + negative test coverage.
- `astrid plan add-step / edit-step / remove-step / supersede-step` round-trip end-to-end on a freshly-created run.
- `astrid plan supersede-step` without `--scope` is rejected at argparse.
- `astrid plan edit-step` / `remove-step` on a dispatched step is rejected at the verb.
- `astrid plan remove-step` on an undispatched step writes a tombstone event; cursor skips it.
- `astrid claim` / `astrid unclaim` emit events; read-only session is blocked.
- `astrid ack` with neither `--agent` nor `--actor` is rejected at argparse.
- `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab, reattach, `astrid next` picks up.
- `manual` adapter: dispatch + ack-driven completion + inbox-driven completion all work.
- `remote-artifact` adapter: rejected at `astrid next` with the deferral message.
- Hype spike: `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` exists with at least 3 enumerated gaps + the schema revisions that landed in response.
- Migrations: `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_plans.py --dry-run` and `--apply` both clean against `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/`. `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_step_paths.py` likewise.

## Useful repo references

- `astrid/core/task/plan.py` — current step schema; will be rewritten.
- `astrid/core/task/events.py` — `append_event_locked` from Sprint 1; reuse for `plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`.
- `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` — current inbox; rewrite for path+version matching.
- `astrid/core/session/lease.py` — Sprint 1's lease with `writer_epoch`. CAS lives here; don't reimplement.
- `astrid/core/timeline/` — Sprint 2's package; do not modify in this sprint.
- `astrid/packs/builtin/hype/` — the spike's target; do NOT modify the real pack, only port to a scratch branch.
- `idea.md` § Sprint 3 (lines 214–295) — canonical sprint definition.
- `idea.md` § Foundation > Load-bearing decisions — the why behind these decisions.

## Style notes

- Type hints + dataclasses.
- Pathlib over os.path.
- Migration scripts: shebang, `chmod +x`, `--dry-run` default.
- All new modules include a one-line docstring.
- New tests follow existing test naming + structure conventions.
- For new event kinds (`plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`, `step_dispatched`): use `append_event_locked` from S1 — every emit is hash-chained and lease-CAS-protected.
- For supersede scope: model the three values as a string Literal (or enum), not a bare string. Argparse rejects unknown values.

## Megaplan-specific guidance

- This sprint is profile `premium/robust/high` because: (1) the kernel pieces here are downstream-cascading — every later sprint depends on these contracts; (2) the six-invariant validator is exactly the sort of contract that benefits from extra critique passes; (3) the hype spike loop has real architectural risk that wants high-depth planning.
- If the hype spike surfaces a stop-line, halt the sprint rather than push through. Document the gap and stop for human review.
- Use `astrid plan add-step` for dogfooding any sub-tasks of this sprint that are worth tracking — friction felt here is friction every user feels.

        Plan:

# Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — Step model overhaul, mutable plans, location-agnostic execution

## Overview

Sprint 3 collapses the Astrid step model into a single `Step` shape, introduces mid-run plan-mutation verbs gated by a six-invariant validator, versioned step paths (`steps/<id>/v<N>/…`), three execution adapters (`local` + `manual` ship; `remote-artifact` rejects), the `claim` verb, ack identity enforcement, optional `cost` on completion events, and inbox path+version routing. Branch `reshape/sprint-3` is already checked out clean; Sprint 0/1/2 surfaces are frozen.

This is iteration 3. The structure (Phase 0 settled decisions + 8 implementation phases) is unchanged; iteration 3 fixes a verified factual error in the v2 call-site sweep and tightens 9 remaining specifics.

**Verified factual correction**: `astrid/core/orchestrator/schema.py:27,494` DOES import + re-export `TASK_STEP_KINDS` (v2's grep claim was wrong; verified by re-running `grep -rn TASK_STEP_KINDS astrid/`). `schema.py` only re-exports the constant — none of `OrchestratorDefinition`'s downstream callers consume it (verified by grepping importers of `astrid.core.orchestrator.schema`). Phase 1 Step 1 now enumerates this file; the fix is to drop the import + the `"TASK_STEP_KINDS"` entry from `schema.py`'s `__all__` alongside removing the constant from `plan.py`. **No other `astrid/core/orchestrator/*` file references the legacy step kinds** (verified by `grep -rn "CodeStep\|AttestedStep\|NestedStep\|TaskPlanStep" astrid/core/orchestrator/`).

Other v3 tightenings (each addresses a v2-open flag — see flags_addressed):

- **Migration diff log specifically flags assignee-broadening** for any-agent/any-human entries (issue_hints).
- **Optional-key omission canonicalization** is enforced for new events too (correctness-2): never serialise as `null` — fields are omitted, period. New compat test covers new-vs-new optional-key absence as well as old-vs-new.
- **`_reject_orchestrators_run` ordering** explicitly placed in the post-construction validator path (`_validate_step`) — NOT `__post_init__` — so the adapter field is parsed before the guard fires (correctness-3).
- **migrate_plans dual-reader** explicitly required: a private `_read_legacy_plan_payload()` bypasses the v2 schema gate; public `load_plan()` keeps rejecting `version != 2` (correctness-4).
- **Inbox migration nested-plan ambiguity** explicitly tested: a legacy `step_id`-only entry whose ID exists at multiple frame depths in a nested plan routes to `.rejected/` with a clear cross-frame-collision reason (scope-2).
- **`plan` verb argparse subparsers**: `plan_verbs.py` exposes a `build_parser()` so `astrid plan --help`, `astrid plan add-step --help`, etc. all surface real help (all_locations-2).
- **cmd_ack Python-level caller audit**: Step 16 also greps for `from .lifecycle_ack import cmd_ack` / `lifecycle_ack.cmd_ack` — Python callers that synthesise `argparse.Namespace` directly bypass the `required=True` flip and need explicit identity assertion (callers-1).
- **Phase 1–3 no-v2 invariant**: an explicit guard test enforces that no v2 step can exist before Phase 3 — mutation verbs that produce v2 don't ship until Phase 3, and a test in Phase 1 asserts the stub adapters cannot construct a `version > 1` step until Step 9's cursor record is in place (callers-2 / FLAG-S3-015).
- **Subjective-judgment criteria** swept: every `must` criterion uses only CI-runnable `requires` (`run_shell`, `run_tests`, `read_files`, `parse_diff`); subjective-judgment items remain only at `should`/`info` (criteria_quality).

## Phase 0: Settled decisions (locked from v2)

SD-A through SD-L from v2 carry forward unchanged. Iteration 3 adds two refinements that don't reopen prior decisions:

- **SD-A refinement (assignee broadening)**: legacy attested → `any-agent`/`any-human` mapping is preserved; the migration diff log MUST emit a top-of-file banner listing every step whose assignee was broadened, with the recommendation to pin via `astrid claim` after migration. Tests assert the banner exists when at least one assignee was broadened.
- **SD-J refinement ([REDACTED] for new events)**: `make_step_dispatched_event` and the `cost` field on completion events use **omit-when-absent** semantics for ALL optional keys (`adapter`, `step_version`, `pid`, `cost`). Two NEW events with the same logical content but one declaring `pid` and the other not produce different (correct) hashes; two NEW events both omitting `pid` produce identical hashes regardless of code path. The compat test exercises both old-vs-new AND new-vs-new optional-key omission.

(Settled decisions SD-A through SD-L themselves — assignee mapping, group-step `requires_ack`, manual dispatch payload, plan-verb namespace, cost omitted-when-undeclared, `TaskPlan.version` 1→2, `instructions` field, event-derived cursor, single-emission ownership, event-factory backward compat, `_reject_orchestrators_run` scoping, group-step supersede children inheritance — are restated in v2's plan body and remain authoritative; not re-debated here.)

## Phase 1: Draft schema + path helpers + adapter ABC stub (the keystone, unlocked)

### Step 1: Audit current step model + COMPLETE call-site sweep (`astrid/core/task/plan.py`, `astrid/core/task/inbox.py`, `astrid/core/task/gate.py`, `astrid/orchestrate/cli.py`, `astrid/orchestrate/test_runner.py`, `astrid/core/orchestrator/schema.py`, `astrid/core/task/__init__.py`, `astrid/core/project/run.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** the current step union (`astrid/core/task/plan.py:17,57,86`) and external references: `iter_steps_with_path` (`plan.py:155`), `step_dir_for_path` (`plan.py:115`), `_validate_*_step` family, `_assert_unique_paths` (`plan.py:556`), `_reject_orchestrators_run` (`plan.py:530`).
2. **Enumerate every importer** of `CodeStep | AttestedStep | NestedStep | TaskPlanStep | TASK_STEP_KINDS` (verified by re-running grep — 8 files):
   - `astrid/core/task/plan.py` (rewrite),
   - `astrid/core/task/lifecycle.py`, `lifecycle_ack.py`, `gate.py`, `inbox.py` (call-site updates),
   - `astrid/orchestrate/cli.py:26-28` + `:142,150,394,398,404,434` (isinstance branches → dispatch on `step.adapter`),
   - `astrid/orchestrate/test_runner.py:40-41` + `:131,153` (same),
   - **`astrid/core/orchestrator/schema.py:27` (imports `TASK_STEP_KINDS`) and `:494` (re-exports in `__all__`) — VERIFIED PRESENT (v2's grep miss, now corrected). Fix: drop both lines. None of `schema.py`'s downstream callers consume the re-exported constant — verified by grepping importers of `astrid.core.orchestrator.schema` (`folder.py`, 4 test files; none reference `TASK_STEP_KINDS`).**
3. **Enumerate `step_dir_for_path` callers** beyond the task package:
   - `astrid/core/task/__init__.py:70,116-117` (re-exports — pass-through, no body to update),
   - `astrid/core/project/run.py:11,81` — `step_dir_for(...)`. Add `step_version=1` default during Phase 1; flip to required after Phase 3 cursor record lands.
4. **Confirm by grep** that no other `astrid/core/orchestrator/*` file references the legacy step kinds. (Verified clean — `schema.py` is the only orchestrator file; the constant is only re-exported, not consumed.)
5. **Map** the cursor's current contract — `gate.peek_current_step` is event-derived from `events.jsonl`. Confirm the new `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` model stays event-derived (SD-H); no new on-disk artifact.

### Step 2: Draft the collapsed `Step` dataclass (`astrid/core/task/plan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Replace** the three step dataclasses with one frozen `Step` per idea.md + SD-G:
   - `id: str`, `version: int = 1`, `adapter: Literal["local","manual","remote-artifact"]`,
   - `requires_ack: bool = False`, `assignee: str = "system"`,
   - `produces: tuple[ProducesEntry, ...] = ()`, `repeat: Repeat | None = None`,
   - `command: str | None = None`, `instructions: str | None = None` (SD-G),
   - `children: tuple[Step, ...] | None = None`,
   - `cost: CostEntry | None = None`, `superseded_by: SupersededRef | None = None`.
2. **`__post_init__` invariants** (structural, not semantic):
   - `command XOR children` (leaf has command, group has children).
   - `assignee` parser validates the five forms.
   - **NO adapter-conditional logic in `__post_init__`** — adapter-conditional checks (e.g. `_reject_orchestrators_run` for `local`) live in the post-construction `_validate_step` function (correctness-3).
3. **`_reject_orchestrators_run` placement** (correctness-3): kept as a free function called from `_validate_step` AFTER the dataclass is constructed. The check reads `step.adapter == "local"` then runs the existing argv-tokenize guard. For `adapter == "manual"` the guard is bypassed (SD-K). Function signature changes from `_reject_orchestrators_run(command, index)` to `_reject_orchestrators_run(step)`.
4. **Bump `TaskPlan.version` to 2** (SD-F): `_validate_plan` rejects `version != 2`. This is the migration discriminator.
5. **Preserve** `_assert_unique_paths`, `parse_from_ref`, `iter_steps_with_path` (rewritten over `Step.children`), the `produces` dict validator, and repeat validators.
6. **Remove `TASK_STEP_KINDS` constant** at `plan.py:17` (and the kind-error message at `:283`). Coordinate with Step 1's removal of `schema.py:27,494`.
7. **Mark** the schema as *draft* in the module docstring until the hype spike has fed back.
8. **Phase-1 invariant test** (callers-2 / FLAG-S3-015): add a unit test in `tests/task/test_plan_schema_collapsed.py` asserting that `Step(version=2, ...)` raises (since mutation verbs that legitimately produce v2 don't ship until Phase 3). The schema accepts only `version == 1` until Step 9 lands; this prevents accidental v2 construction during the Phase 1–3 window.

### Step 3: Versioned step paths with phased call-site rollout (`astrid/core/task/plan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Update** `step_dir_for_path` to accept `step_version: int = 1`. The `int = 1` default is safe through Phase 1–2 because Step 2.8's invariant prevents any v2 step from existing.
2. **Sweep callers**: `gate.py:711,999,1010,1133`, `lifecycle.py:594`, `lifecycle_ack.py`, `inbox.py:207`, `events.py`, `astrid/core/project/run.py:81` (re-exported via `astrid/core/task/__init__.py:70`). Each call passes `step_version` from cursor state; default-=1 falls through during the migration window.

### Step 4: Adapter ABC + result types (`astrid/core/adapter/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `astrid/core/adapter/__init__.py` with `Adapter` Protocol shaped to `cmd_next`'s flow:
   - `dispatch(step: Step, run_ctx: RunContext) -> DispatchResult`
   - `poll(step: Step, run_ctx: RunContext) -> PollResult`
   - `complete(step: Step, run_ctx: RunContext) -> CompleteResult` — returns `CompleteResult{status, returncode?, cost?, reason?}` (SD-I).
2. **Adapters never emit events directly** (SD-I).
3. **Stub** `local.py`, `manual.py`, `remote_artifact.py` with `NotImplementedError` placeholders.

## Phase 3: Validator + mutation verbs + cursor + inbox

### Step 7: Single-entry validator (`astrid/core/task/validator.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `validator.py` exporting `validate_mutation(prior, proposed, *, lease_epoch_actual, lease_epoch_expected) -> None` raising `MutationInvariantError(invariant_id, element, reason)`.
2. **All six invariants** against the proposed effective tree (I1 schema, I2 sibling-id uniqueness, I3 produces-ref integrity, I4 adapter validation, I5 repeat-source resolution, I6 lease-epoch CAS).
3. **Stable invariant-id enum**.

### Step 8: Plan-mutation verbs with argparse build_parser (`astrid/core/task/plan_verbs.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `plan_verbs.py` exporting `cmd_plan_add_step`, `cmd_plan_edit_step`, `cmd_plan_remove_step`, `cmd_plan_supersede_step`, **PLUS `build_parser()` exposing argparse subparsers for the four sub-verbs and their flags** (all_locations-2). Mirrors the `astrid/core/session/cli.py` `build_parser` pattern. `astrid plan --help` → list of sub-verbs; `astrid plan add-step --help` → flags.
2. **Each verb**: load `plan.json` + replay → current tree; compute proposed tree; `validate_mutation` under writer_epoch CAS; emit one `plan_mutated` event via `append_event_locked`.
3. **Dispatched-step gates**: edit/remove on dispatched → rejected; remove on undispatched → tombstone; supersede without `--scope` → argparse `choices=["all","future-iterations","future-items"]`.
4. **Group-step supersede semantics** (SD-L): scope=future-iterations on a mid-iteration group → new v<N+1> children replace v<N> on next iteration. Documented in verb docstring + tested in `test_cursor_supersede_scopes.py`.
5. **Effective-tree replay**: shared helper `apply_mutations(plan, events) -> TaskPlan`.

### Step 9: Cursor honoring versions + tombstones + scopes (`astrid/core/task/gate.py`, `astrid/core/task/lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend the event-derived cursor** to `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` (SD-H). No new on-disk file.
2. **Walk** the effective plan respecting tombstones, scope=all, scope=future-iterations / future-items.
3. **`astrid status`** surfaces the cursor's current step at its current version.
4. **Now flip `step_dir_for_path`'s `step_version` to required** AND remove the Step 2.8 v2-rejection invariant (callers-2 / FLAG-S3-015). After this step lands, v2 steps can legitimately exist; the ordering invariant has done its job and is dropped.

### Step 10: Inbox path+version routing + identity-on-inbox (`astrid/core/task/inbox.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Rewrite** `_parse_entry` and `consume_inbox_entry` to match on `(plan_step_path, step_version, item_id?)`. New schema bumps `schema_version: 2` on inbox JSON; absence is treated as legacy (handled by Step 18a migration).
2. **Identity enforcement parity**: inbox entries for manual-adapter completion MUST carry `submitted_by` AND `submitted_by_kind: "agent" | "actor"`. Missing identity → `.rejected/` with clear reason.
3. **Stale entries** → `.rejected/<sha256>`. **No entry vanishes silently** (stop-line).

## Phase 4: Adapters wired into `cmd_next`

### Step 11: Local adapter (`astrid/core/adapter/local.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`dispatch`** spawns subprocess in project root, returns `DispatchResult{pid, started_at}`.
2. **`complete`** returns `CompleteResult{status, returncode, ...}` per exit-code + produces-check rules. Cost typically omitted (not null — SD-E + SD-J refinement).
3. **Subprocess-outlives-tab**: detach-capable; reattach + `astrid next` resumes.

### Step 12: Manual adapter (`astrid/core/adapter/manual.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`dispatch`** writes `command` (+ `instructions`) payload to `runs/<run>/steps/<id>/v<N>/dispatch.json` (SD-C).
2. **`complete`** via `astrid ack` (sync) OR inbox JSON (async, with required `submitted_by_kind`).

### Step 13: Remote-artifact adapter (`astrid/core/adapter/remote_artifact.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`dispatch`** raises with the exact deferral string. `cmd_next` catches and exits non-zero.

### Step 14: Wire `cmd_next` to dispatch through adapters; emission ownership (`astrid/core/task/lifecycle.py`, `astrid/core/task/gate.py`, `astrid/core/task/events.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Replace** the hard-coded subprocess assumption in `cmd_next` with adapter resolution from `step.adapter`.
2. **Single emission point** (SD-I): `cmd_next` emits `step_dispatched`; `gate.record_dispatch_complete` emits `step_completed`/`step_failed`. Adapters never call `append_event_locked`.
3. **`make_step_dispatched_event` extension** (SD-J + refinement): signature becomes `make_step_dispatched_event(plan_step_path, command, *, adapter=None, step_version=None, pid=None)`. **Implementation rule: optional kwargs that are `None` are OMITTED from the returned dict, never serialised as `null`.** This makes new-vs-new hash-stable when both omit the same optional fields. Old events stay hash-stable too (they never had these keys).
4. **`requires_ack` enforcement**: cursor stays on step until non-stale ack. Group steps with `requires_ack=true` block on group's path until ack.
5. **Cost**: completion events include `cost` only when declared; omitted otherwise (SD-E).

Any test that synthesises `argparse.Namespace(agent=None, actor=None, ...)` and calls `cmd_ack` directly bypasses the argparse rejection — those need explicit assertion at the function boundary (Namespace check inside `cmd_ack` rejecting None/None and both-set).
3. **Carry** `writer_epoch` from lease read; reject stale acks.

**Scope:** Medium

The public `load_plan()` continues to enforce `version == 2`. The legacy reader is private to the migration script and the legacy test fixtures; not exposed from `astrid/core/task/plan.py`'s public API.
3. **Walk** `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/`. For each `plan.json`:
   - `code` → `adapter:"local"`, `version:1`, `assignee:"system"`,
   - `attested` → `adapter:"manual"`, `command` preserved, `instructions` mapped verbatim (SD-G), `requires_ack:true`, `assignee` per SD-A,

   - **Bump `TaskPlan.version` 1 → 2**.

Run \`astrid claim <step> --for ...\` post-migration to pin a concrete identity.` The banner enumerates each broadened step by path. Tests assert the banner appears whenever ≥1 attested step is migrated.

6. **Diff log**: `plan.migration.log` documents every decision.

### Step 18a: Inbox-entry migration with cross-frame ambiguity handling (`scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_inbox.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **NEW** — addresses FLAG-S3-005. Walks `runs/*/inbox/*.json` and rewrites legacy `step_id`-only entries to `(plan_step_path, step_version: 1, schema_version: 2)`.
2. **Path resolution** (scope-2): walks the effective tree (post-plan-migration). For each legacy entry:
   - Resolve `step_id` to a `plan_step_path` by full-tree walk.
   - **If 0 matches**: route to `.rejected/` with reason `step_id_not_found`.
   - **If 1 match** (any depth): rewrite.
   - **If >1 match** (sibling collision OR cross-frame ambiguity — same `step_id` at multiple depths in nested plans): route to `.rejected/` with reason `step_id_ambiguous_at_<n>_paths` listing the candidates.
3. **Idempotent**: entries with `schema_version: 2` are no-ops.
4. **`--dry-run` default**.

**Scope:** Medium
1. Walk `runs/*/steps/<id>/*` → `runs/*/steps/<id>/v1/*`.
2. Idempotent (skip dirs with existing `v<N>/` child).
3. Empty workspace exits 0.

8. **`test_step_dispatched_event_compat.py`** (correctness-2 / SD-J refinement) — three scenarios:
   - **Old-vs-new interop**: old payload (no adapter/step_version/pid) and new payload (with all three) replay correctly side-by-side.
   - **New-vs-new omission stability**: two new payloads, both omitting `pid` (e.g. manual adapter, group step), produce identical hashes.
   - **Null-vs-omit divergence**: a payload with `pid: null` would NOT hash-match an omitted-pid payload — the factory MUST omit, never serialise null. Test asserts the factory's output dict has no key for `pid` when `pid is None`.

1. **Phase 0** decisions locked (no code).
2. **Phase 1** (schema draft + path helpers + adapter ABC stub + COMPLETE call-site sweep including `astrid/core/orchestrator/schema.py:27,494` removal + Phase 1–3 v2-rejection invariant in `Step.__post_init__`).

1. **Targeted unit tests** per module: schema, validator, mutation verbs, cursor, inbox.

      "reason": "Verified by re-running grep that `astrid/core/orchestrator/schema.py:27,494` imports and re-exports `TASK_STEP_KINDS`. Phase 1 Step 1 now enumerates this file explicitly. Fix specified: drop the import + the `\"TASK_STEP_KINDS\"` entry from `__all__`. Verified no downstream caller of `astrid.core.orchestrator.schema` consumes the re-exported constant (`folder.py` + 4 test files import other names; `tests/test_task_plan_schema.py:8` imports it but is being rewritten in Step 23)."
    },
    {
      "id": "issue_hints",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "SD-A refinement in Phase 0 requires the migration diff log to emit an assignee-broadening banner: enumerates each broadened step by path and recommends `astrid claim <step> --for ...` to pin concrete identity. Step 18.4 specifies the banner; Step 22.1 asserts the banner appears when \u22651 attested step is migrated."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-1",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Same as FLAG-S3-003 \u2014 the factual claim that `astrid/core/orchestrator/*` is grep-clean was wrong; v3 corrects it explicitly with the verified grep result and lists the file in the Phase 1 Step 1 sweep."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-2",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "SD-J refinement in Phase 0 specifies omit-when-None semantics for ALL optional event-factory kwargs (adapter, step_version, pid, cost). Step 14.3 says `make_step_dispatched_event` MUST omit (not serialise as null) any None-valued optional kwarg. Step 20.8 expands the compat test to three scenarios: old-vs-new interop, new-vs-new omission stability (two new payloads both omitting pid hash identically), null-vs-omit divergence (a payload with pid:null would NOT hash-match an omitted-pid payload \u2014 factory test asserts the dict has no `pid` key when pid is None)."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-3",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Step 2.2 explicitly forbids adapter-conditional logic in `__post_init__` (which only enforces structural invariants like command XOR children). Step 2.3 places `_reject_orchestrators_run` in the post-construction `_validate_step` path with a new signature that takes the constructed Step and reads `step.adapter == \"local\"` first. Ordering is enforced by the validation pipeline: dataclass construction \u2192 `_validate_step` \u2192 adapter-conditional guards."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-4",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Step 18.2 explicitly requires a dual-reader: `migrate_plans.py` imports a private `_read_legacy_plan_payload(path)` helper that loads + JSON-parses without running `_validate_plan` (which now rejects version!=2). Public `load_plan()` keeps rejecting. The legacy reader is private to the migration script + legacy test fixtures, not exposed from `astrid/core/task/plan.py`'s public API."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-5",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Already addressed in v2 (SD-J + Step 14.3); v3 adds the new-vs-new omission stability test scenario in Step 20.8 to close the remaining gap correctness-2 raised."
    },
    {
      "id": "scope-1",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Same as FLAG-S3-003 / correctness-1 \u2014 `astrid/core/orchestrator/schema.py` is now explicitly enumerated in Phase 1 Step 1 with the specific fix (drop import at :27, drop re-export at :494)."
    },
    {
      "id": "scope-2",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Step 18a.2 explicitly handles three resolution cases (0 matches, 1 match, >1 matches). The >1 case covers BOTH sibling collisions AND cross-frame ambiguity (same step_id at multiple depths in nested plans). Routes to `.rejected/` with reason `step_id_ambiguous_at_<n>_paths` enumerating candidates. Step 22.2 adds an explicit cross-frame ambiguity test fixture."
    },
    {
      "id": "scope-3",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Already addressed in v2 (SD-H \u2014 cursor is event-derived, no on-disk artifact); no change needed in v3."

    {
      "id": "all_locations-1",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Same as FLAG-S3-003 \u2014 `astrid/core/orchestrator/schema.py:27,494` enumerated in Phase 1 Step 1; Step 23.1 also calls out updating `tests/test_task_plan_schema.py:8` which imports the constant via the schema re-export."
    },
    {
      "id": "all_locations-2",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Step 8.1 expands plan_verbs.py to expose a `build_parser()` providing argparse subparsers for the four sub-verbs and their flags (mirrors `astrid/core/session/cli.py`).     },
    {
      "id": "callers-1",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Step 16.2 splits the caller audit into two passes: (a) CLI shape via grep `astrid ack` across packs/, tests/, hook.py, docs/; (b) Python shape via grep `from astrid.core.task.lifecycle_ack import` and `lifecycle_ack.cmd_ack` across astrid/ and tests/. Any test that synthesises argparse.Namespace directly bypasses the argparse rejection \u2014 cmd_ack adds a function-boundary identity assertion. Step 20.7 adds a Python-level test calling cmd_ack(Namespace(agent=None, actor=None)) to assert a clean error."
    },
    {
      "id": "callers-2",
      "resolution": "addressed",

    "`make_step_dispatched_event` and the cost field on completion events use omit-when-None semantics across the board. Optional fields are NEVER serialised as `null`; they are absent from the dict. New event compat test asserts this invariant explicitly.",

    "iteration": 3,
    "idea": "# Sprint 3 \u2014 Step model overhaul, mutable plans, location-agnostic execution\n\nAstrid reshape sprint 3. Full design: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/idea.md` \u00a7 Sprint 3 (lines 214\u2013295). This brief expands the sprint section for execution. **This is a large sprint and the architectural keystone of the reshape \u2014 premium/robust/high profile picked deliberately.**\n\nWorking directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid`. Start from branch `reshape/sprint-2`; create `reshape/sprint-3` off it and work there.\n\n## Goal\n\nCollapse the step model into one shape (no more `code`/`attested`/`nested` kinds). Make plans editable mid-run with explicit mutation rules. Make execution location-agnostic via three adapters (only `local` and `manual` ship this sprint; `remote-artifact` is reserved and rejected at runtime). Add the `claim` verb. Land the kernel-level pieces (validator, versioned step paths, mutation events, inbox path+version matching) that Sprint 4 + 5a all depend on.\n\n**No orchestrator porting (Sprint 5a/5b territory).** Sprint 3 is the kernel + the adapters; existing packs are migrated forward mechanically.\n\n## Foundational decisions (locked, do not re-debate)\n\n### The new Step shape\n\n```\nStep {\n  id: str\n  version: int                       # bumped when a dispatched step is superseded\n  adapter: \"local\" | \"manual\" | \"remote-artifact\"\n  requires_ack: bool\n  assignee: \"system\" | \"agent:<id>\" | \"any-agent\" | \"human:<name>\" | \"any-human\"\n  produces: [{name, path, check}]\n  repeat: until | for_each | none\n  command: str?                      # leaf step: the thing to run; interpreted by adapter\n  children: [Step]?                  # group step: nesting; replaces old `nested` kind\n  cost: {amount, currency, source}?  # recorded at completion\n  superseded_by: {\n    to_version: int,\n    scope: \"all\" | \"future-iterations\" | \"future-items\"  # REQUIRED; no default\n  }?\n}\n```\n\n- Leaf step has `command`, no `children`.\n- Group step has `children`, no `command`. Its `produces` aggregates from descendants.\n- `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are **gone** (migrated forward \u2014 see Migration).\n- `version` starts at 1 for the initial step; bumps to 2+ on supersede.\n\n### Supersession scope is mandatory\n\nA `supersede-step` verb without an explicit `scope` is rejected at the verb. Three scopes:\n- `\"all\"` \u2014 aborts in-flight iterations/items and restarts at iter/item 1 of the new version.\n- `\"future-iterations\"` \u2014 `repeat.until` only; current iteration finishes against old version, iter+1 uses new version.\n- `\"future-items\"` \u2014 `repeat.for_each` only; current item finishes against old version, next item uses new version.\n\n### Plan mutation rules\n\n- `plan.json` is the *initial* plan, written once at run start.\n- The *effective* plan = `plan.json` + replayed mutation events from `events.jsonl`.\n- **Append-only once dispatched.** A step is \"dispatched\" the moment `astrid next` returns its payload (event: `step_dispatched`). After dispatch:\n  - `remove-step` is rejected. Use `abort` on the run instead, or `supersede-step`.\n  - `edit-step` is rejected. Use `supersede-step` to bump version.\n  - The original `steps/<id>/v1/` directory is **never deleted**.\n- **Undispatched steps** can be added, edited, or removed freely. `remove-step` writes a tombstone event; the cursor skips tombstoned steps.\n- **Canonical step path is always versioned**: every step lives at `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`. Iterations and items live under `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/` and `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`.\n\n### The six plan-mutation invariants\n\nSingle function (call it `validate_mutation` or similar) runs **all six** invariants against the proposed effective tree:\n\n1. **Schema validation** \u2014 full re-validate of the proposed effective tree (not a diff).\n2. **Sibling-id uniqueness** at every frame (existing `plan.py:_assert_unique_paths` rule, applied to effective tree).\n3. **`produces` reference integrity** \u2014 no dangling refs to deleted/tombstoned steps.\n4. **Adapter validation** \u2014 declared adapter is `local | manual | remote-artifact`, command shape matches adapter (e.g. `manual` cannot have empty `command` string).\n5. **Repeat-source validation** \u2014 `repeat.for_each.from_ref` resolves to a real prior `produces` in the effective tree.\n6. **Lease epoch CAS** \u2014 the writer's epoch matches current (Sprint 1's lease).\n\nMutations that fail **any** check are rejected at the verb. No partial application. The verb returns a non-zero exit with the specific invariant that failed.\n\n### Three adapters; only two ship this sprint\n\n- **`local`** \u2014 `command` is a shell command run as a subprocess in the project root. Outputs land in `produces/`. Completion = exit code 0 + produces checks pass.\n- **`manual`** \u2014 agent (or human) runs the work out-of-band; `command` is the dispatch payload (instructions). The agent is responsible for landing outputs in `produces/`. Completion = `astrid ack` (sync) or completion JSON in inbox (async).\n- **`remote-artifact`** \u2014 schema-reserved here, **rejected at runtime** in Sprint 3 with: `astrid start / astrid next: step '<id>' declares adapter 'remote-artifact'; not yet implemented (Sprint 5a). Use --adapter local or manual.` Full implementation lands in Sprint 5a alongside the hype port.\n\nAdapters are first-class code, not config.\n\n### Ack identity at ack time, not author time\n\n`astrid ack <step> --decision {approve,retry,iterate,abort} --agent <id>|--actor <name> [--evidence <path>]`. Both `--agent` and `--actor` are required (one or the other) \u2014 **no anonymous acks**. Every ack carries the `writer_epoch` it read; stale acks are rejected by the kernel.\n\n### Authoring split\n\n- **Leaf-template** \u2014 what we used to call an executor. One concrete unit of work. Same pack layout on disk (`pack/<name>/run.py` + `executor.yaml`).\n- **Plan-template** \u2014 what we used to call an orchestrator: a reusable composition. Materializes into a step subtree at plan-build or plan-mutation time.\n\nRuntime never sees templates \u2014 only the resulting step tree. During Sprint 3, the new template kinds land **alongside** the legacy `OrchestratorDefinition` YAML \u2014 both readable. Legacy YAML is materialized into the new step tree on the way in. (Sprint 5b removes legacy YAML acceptance.)\n\n### Capture-everything rule\n\nAnything producing an audit-worthy artifact = a step. The friction is small: `astrid plan add-step --command '...' --produces '...'` + `astrid next`. The alternative \u2014 agent runs python ad-hoc, artifact appears with no provenance \u2014 is what we're eliminating. Truly ephemeral commands don't need it.\n\n## Deliverables\n\n### Step schema + plan rewrite\n\n- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/plan.py` against the new step shape. Keep `_assert_unique_paths` (or its equivalent in the new code) and the validators for the existing invariants; add what's needed for the new ones.\n- Versioned step path everywhere: `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`. Update all path helpers in `astrid/core/task/`.\n- **`code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are gone.** Migration script (see below) rewrites existing plans.\n\n### Plan-mutation verbs + event\n\n- `astrid plan add-step` \u2014 insert a step into the effective tree at a given path. Position via `--after <step-path>` / `--before <step-path>` / `--into <group-path>`.\n- `astrid plan edit-step` \u2014 modify an undispatched step (fields specifiable as flags).\n- `astrid plan remove-step` \u2014 write a tombstone event for an undispatched step. Rejected on dispatched steps.\n- `astrid plan supersede-step` \u2014 bump version on a dispatched (or any) step. **Requires `--scope {all,future-iterations,future-items}`.**\n- All four verbs emit a single `plan_mutated` event in `events.jsonl` carrying: actor, writer_epoch, diff (the specific operation), timestamp. The hash chain extends to plan mutations.\n\n### Plan-mutation validator\n\n- Single entry point that runs all six invariants. Every verb routes through it. Rejection is total.\n- Returns structured error: which invariant failed + the specific element that triggered it.\n- Test: each invariant has at least one positive (passes) and one negative (rejects with the right error code) case.\n\n### Cursor\n\n- Cursor stores `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` per dispatched step.\n- Walks the effective plan respecting tombstones and supersedes (honoring `scope` per repeat semantics).\n- `astrid status` shows the cursor's current step at its current version.\n\n### Inbox path+version match\n\n- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` so entries match on `(plan_step_path, step_version, item_id?)` not just the leaf step id.\n- Inbox entries from before a supersede event route to the original version's directory; entries after route to the new version.\n- Stale entries that target a tombstoned or fully-superseded step move to `.rejected/` (existing pattern \u2014 extend it).\n\n### Adapters\n\n- `astrid/core/adapter/local.py` \u2014 subprocess-based local execution. Dispatch = spawn subprocess (sync or detached); completion = exit code 0 + produces checks; failure = non-zero exit OR missing produces.\n- `astrid/core/adapter/manual.py` \u2014 emit dispatch payload (`command` field) to the agent's display path; completion = `astrid ack` event OR completion JSON arriving in inbox.\n- `astrid/core/adapter/remote_artifact.py` \u2014 stub that rejects at dispatch time with the deferral message above.\n- All three implement a common interface (e.g. `Adapter.dispatch(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.poll(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.complete(step, run_ctx)`). Pick the interface to fit how `astrid next` already works.\n\n### Claim verb\n\n- `astrid claim <step> [--for human:<name>] [--for agent:<id>]`. Default `--for` = current session's identity.\n- Emits `claim` / `unclaim` events.\n- Read-only sessions blocked from claiming.\n\n### `requires_ack` enforcement\n\n- Steps with `requires_ack: true` cannot complete on adapter completion alone; require an `astrid ack` event with a non-stale `writer_epoch`.\n- `--agent` or `--actor` is **required** at ack time. Argparse rejects neither/both.\n\n### Mutating verbs include `writer_epoch` + CAS\n\n- Every mutating verb reads the current lease's `writer_epoch` before mutating; bumps it as part of the mutation; rejects if the epoch advanced under it.\n- Relies on Sprint 1's lease primitives directly \u2014 do NOT reimplement.\n\n### Cost field on completion events\n\n- Each step completion event (`step_completed`, `step_failed`) carries `cost: {amount, currency, source}` if the adapter or executing code declared one.\n- Adapters MAY emit cost; local subprocess execution typically won't. The field is optional but the schema reserves it (Sprint 4 + 5a depend on this).\n\n### Inbox-driven async-completion documented + exercised\n\n- Worked example for `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab. Demonstrate that closing the tab, opening a new one, `astrid attach`, `astrid next` picks up wherever inbox left things.\n- Worked example for `manual` adapter: out-of-band ack landing as a completion JSON in inbox.\n- Documentation lives at `docs/reshape/sprint-3/async-completion.md`.\n\n### Migrations\n\n- **Plan migration** \u2014 every existing `plan.json` rewritten to the collapsed schema. Every existing step gets `adapter: local` and `version: 1`. `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds rewritten to the new shape (best-effort mapping; ambiguous cases get `adapter: local` + a TODO note in the diff log).\n- **Step path migration** \u2014 every existing `steps/<id>/...` directory becomes `steps/<id>/v1/...` in one shot. Run on `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/` (which is currently mostly empty \u2014 but the script must be idempotent and exercise the no-op path cleanly).\n- Both migrations live under `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/`. `--dry-run` default; `--apply` commits.\n\n### Hype port spike \u2014 back half of sprint, throwaway, do NOT merge to reshape/sprint-3\n\n- Scratch branch `reshape/sprint-3-hype-spike` off `reshape/sprint-3`.\n- Take the first three steps of `builtin.hype` only (transcribe \u2192 cut \u2192 render) and port them against the new step model.\n- Deliverable is `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` enumerating every schema gap, adapter mismatch, and cost-field ambiguity discovered.\n- **Schema revisions resulting from the spike land in Sprint 3, not deferred to a follow-up.** Revise the schema while it's still cheap.\n- The spike branch is throwaway \u2014 push it for archival but do not merge.\n\n### Tests\n\n- `tests/task/test_plan_schema_collapsed.py` \u2014 new step schema validation, including all six invariants positive + negative.\n- `tests/task/test_plan_mutation_verbs.py` \u2014 add/edit/remove/supersede each succeed in the happy path and reject correctly when post-dispatch / scope-missing / invariant-failing.\n- `tests/task/test_step_versioning.py` \u2014 supersede produces `v2/`, `v1/` survives, cursor tracks correct version.\n- `tests/task/test_cursor_supersede_scopes.py` \u2014 all three scopes exercised under repeat.until and repeat.for_each.\n- `tests/task/test_inbox_path_version_match.py` \u2014 entries route correctly across a supersede event; stale entries land in `.rejected/`.\n- `tests/adapter/test_local.py` \u2014 subprocess dispatch, completion, failure modes.\n- `tests/adapter/test_manual.py` \u2014 dispatch payload emission, ack-driven completion, inbox-driven completion.\n- `tests/adapter/test_remote_artifact_rejected.py` \u2014 `astrid next` rejects cleanly with the deferral message.\n- `tests/task/test_claim_unclaim.py` \u2014 claim emits event, unclaim emits event, read-only session blocked.\n- `tests/task/test_ack_identity_required.py` \u2014 argparse rejects no-agent-no-actor; both rejected too.\n- `tests/migrations/test_plan_migration.py` \u2014 collapsed-schema rewrite across all old step kinds.\n- `tests/migrations/test_step_path_migration.py` \u2014 `steps/<id>/...` \u2192 `steps/<id>/v1/...`, idempotence, no-op path.\n- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still pass (regression \u2014 full pytest must come back green-modulo-known-pre-existing-failures).\n\n## Stop-lines\n\n1. **Hype spike reveals plan-template materialization can't express hype's dynamic discovery** (or any step shape is fundamentally wrong for hype). Halt before locking the Sprint 3 schema; redesign rather than push through. Write the redesign to `docs/reshape/sprint-3/schema-redesign.md` and stop for human review.\n2. **Apex contract (S1) regresses under plan mutation events**. Locked event-append + epoch CAS is what plan-mutated rides on; if multi-tab races appear, halt and fix S1's primitives, not S3's surface.\n3. **Inbox path+version routing loses entries silently**. If any inbox entry vanishes (not routed, not rejected, just gone) under any supersede or tombstone path, that's a stop-line \u2014 entries must always have a deterministic destination.\n\n## Out of scope (do NOT touch)\n\n- The `remote-artifact` adapter implementation (Sprint 5a).\n- Orchestrator porting \u2014 hype proper, event_talks, thumbnail_maker (Sprint 5a/5b). The hype spike is a throwaway port of the first three steps only.\n- RunPod lift (Sprint 4).\n- Anything in `astrid/packs/builtin/human_review/`, `astrid/packs/seinfeld/` \u2014 pre-existing WIP, leave untouched.\n- Export / verify / cost aggregation (Sprint 5b).\n- `astrid projects timeline` (the legacy reigh-app subcommand) \u2014 leave it alone in this sprint; rename happens in Sprint 5b.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- `git branch --show-current` returns `reshape/sprint-3`.\n- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still green (modulo the 7 pre-existing unrelated failures verified in S2 \u2014 those are out of scope).\n- `pytest tests/task/ tests/adapter/ tests/migrations/sprint-3/ -v` all green.\n- Plan-mutation validator: every invariant has positive + negative test coverage.\n- `astrid plan add-step / edit-step / remove-step / supersede-step` round-trip end-to-end on a freshly-created run.\n- `astrid plan supersede-step` without `--scope` is rejected at argparse.\n- `astrid plan edit-step` / `remove-step` on a dispatched step is rejected at the verb.\n- `astrid plan remove-step` on an undispatched step writes a tombstone event; cursor skips it.\n- `astrid claim` / `astrid unclaim` emit events; read-only session is blocked.\n- `astrid ack` with neither `--agent` nor `--actor` is rejected at argparse.\n- `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab, reattach, `astrid next` picks up.\n- `manual` adapter: dispatch + ack-driven completion + inbox-driven completion all work.\n- `remote-artifact` adapter: rejected at `astrid next` with the deferral message.\n- Hype spike: `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` exists with at least 3 enumerated gaps + the schema revisions that landed in response.\n- Migrations: `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_plans.py --dry-run` and `--apply` both clean against `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/`. `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_step_paths.py` likewise.\n\n## Useful repo references\n\n- `astrid/core/task/plan.py` \u2014 current step schema; will be rewritten.\n- `astrid/core/task/events.py` \u2014 `append_event_locked` from Sprint 1; reuse for `plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`.\n- `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` \u2014 current inbox; rewrite for path+version matching.\n- `astrid/core/session/lease.py` \u2014 Sprint 1's lease with `writer_epoch`. CAS lives here; don't reimplement.\n- `astrid/core/timeline/` \u2014 Sprint 2's package; do not modify in this sprint.\n- `astrid/packs/builtin/hype/` \u2014 the spike's target; do NOT modify the real pack, only port to a scratch branch.\n- `idea.md` \u00a7 Sprint 3 (lines 214\u2013295) \u2014 canonical sprint definition.\n- `idea.md` \u00a7 Foundation > Load-bearing decisions \u2014 the why behind these decisions.\n\n## Style notes\n\n- Type hints + dataclasses.\n- Pathlib over os.path.\n- Migration scripts: shebang, `chmod +x`, `--dry-run` default.\n- All new modules include a one-line docstring.\n- New tests follow existing test naming + structure conventions.\n- For new event kinds (`plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`, `step_dispatched`): use `append_event_locked` from S1 \u2014 every emit is hash-chained and lease-CAS-protected.\n- For supersede scope: model the three values as a string Literal (or enum), not a bare string. Argparse rejects unknown values.\n\n## Megaplan-specific guidance\n\n- This sprint is profile `premium/robust/high` because: (1) the kernel pieces here are downstream-cascading \u2014 every later sprint depends on these contracts; (2) the six-invariant validator is exactly the sort of contract that benefits from extra critique passes; (3) the hype spike loop has real architectural risk that wants high-depth planning.\n- If the hype spike surfaces a stop-line, halt the sprint rather than push through. Document the gap and stop for human review.\n- Use `astrid plan add-step` for dogfooding any sub-tasks of this sprint that are worth tracking \u2014 friction felt here is friction every user feels.",

Other tightenings: SD-A refinement requires the migration diff log to emit an assignee-broadening banner enumerating broadened steps and recommending `astrid claim` (issue_hints); SD-J refinement specifies omit-when-None semantics for ALL optional event-factory kwargs and adds a new compat test scenario covering new-vs-new optional-key omission AND null-vs-omit divergence (correctness-2); Step 2 places `_reject_orchestrators_run` explicitly in the post-construction `_validate_step` path (NOT `__post_init__`) so adapter is parsed first (correctness-3); Step 18 adds an explicit dual-reader requirement \u2014 private `_read_legacy_plan_payload()` bypasses the v2 schema gate; public `load_plan()` keeps rejecting (correctness-4); Step 18a inbox-migration tests now include explicit cross-frame ambiguity (same step_id at multiple depths in nested plans \u2192 `.rejected/` with explicit reason \u2014 scope-2); Step 8 expands `plan_verbs.py` to include a `build_parser()` exposing argparse subparsers so `astrid plan --help` and `astrid plan add-step --help` work end-to-end (all_locations-2); Step 16 caller audit extended to grep Python imports of `cmd_ack` (not just shell `astrid ack`) plus a Python-boundary identity assertion (callers-1); Step 2.8 adds a Phase 1\u20133 v2-rejection invariant in `Step.__post_init__` that prevents accidental v2-step construction during the Phase 1\u20133 window \u2014 retired in Step 9 once cursor record + mutation verbs land legitimately (callers-2 / FLAG-S3-015); audited all `must` success criteria \u2014 none use `subjective_judgment` (only `should`/`info` items do), so criteria_quality concern is verified resolved."

Added Step 18a inbox-entry migration script + test to address scope-2 silent-rejection risk. Reordered step_dir_for_path's required-version flip to AFTER cursor record lands (Phase 3, not Phase 1). Added ack-caller audit step. Added identity enforcement on manual-adapter inbox completion. Added test for old/new step_dispatched event hash-chain compatibility. This catches CLI-shaped invocations but not Python-level callers of cmd_ack() (e.g., test fixtures that synthesize args.Namespace directly). Plan should grep cmd_ack imports too, not just `astrid ack` shell strings.",
        "resolution": "v3 fixes a verified factual error in v2's call-site sweep (re-ran grep to confirm) and tightens 9 specific flags. The big correction: `astrid/core/orchestrator/schema.py:27,494` DOES import + re-export `TASK_STEP_KINDS` \u2014 v2's claim of grep-clean was wrong. v3 enumerates schema.py in Phase 1 Step 1 and adds the explicit fix (drop the import + the `__all__` entry; verified no downstream caller of `astrid.core.orchestrator.schema` consumes the re-exported constant). Other tightenings: SD-A refinement requires the migration diff log to emit an assignee-broadening banner enumerating broadened steps and recommending `astrid claim` (issue_hints); SD-J refinement specifies omit-when-None semantics for ALL optional event-factory kwargs and adds a new compat test scenario covering new-vs-new optional-key omission AND null-vs-omit divergence (correctness-2); Step 2 places `_reject_orchestrators_run` explicitly in the post-construction `_validate_step` path (NOT `__post_init__`) so adapter is parsed first (correctness-3); Step 18 adds an explicit dual-reader requirement \u2014 private `_read_legacy_plan_payload()` bypasses the v2 schema gate; public `load_plan()` keeps rejecting (correctness-4); Step 18a inbox-migration tests now include explicit cross-frame ambiguity (same step_id at multiple depths in nested plans \u2192 `.rejected/` with explicit reason \u2014 scope-2); Step 8 expands `plan_verbs.py` to include a `build_parser()` exposing argparse subparsers so `astrid plan --help` and `astrid plan add-step --help` work end-to-end (all_locations-2); Step 16 caller audit extended to grep Python imports of `cmd_ack` (not just shell `astrid ack`) plus a Python-boundary identity assertion (callers-1); Step 2.8 adds a Phase 1\u20133 v2-rejection invariant in `Step.__post_init__` that prevents accidental v2-step construction during the Phase 1\u20133 window \u2014 retired in Step 9 once cursor record + mutation verbs land legitimately (callers-2 / FLAG-S3-015); audited all `must` success criteria \u2014 none use `subjective_judgment` (only `should`/`info` items do), so criteria_quality concern is verified resolved."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-S3-010",
        "concern": "Migration assignee broadening (any-agent / any-human) loses authoring fidelity.         "resolution": "SD-A + Step 18.4"

        "concern": "migrate_plans.py needs a dedicated legacy reader because the new validator rejects version!=2; plan does not explicitly call out this dual-reader requirement.",

        "concern": "_reject_orchestrators_run scoping to adapter=='local' (SD-K) doesn't specify when in the Step lifecycle the adapter check occurs vs. command parsing; ordering inside __post_init__ vs. post-construction validator matters.",
        "resolution": "SD-K + Step 2.5"
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-S3-013",
        "concern": "Inbox-migration nested-plan ambiguity: a legacy step_id-only entry with the same id reused across nested frames has multiple possible plan_step_path destinations. Plan addresses sibling collisions but not cross-frame ambiguity.",
        "resolution": "Step 18a + Step 22.2"
      },
      {

        "concern": "Ack-caller audit in Step 16 greps for shell-string `astrid ack` invocations; misses Python-level cmd_ack() callers (e.g., test fixtures synthesizing args.Namespace directly). Should grep cmd_ack imports too.",
